If a duplicate worker already wrote the // result, this invocation returns the already-persisted result. const resultKey = k.actionResult(call.idempotencyKey); const oldResult = await state.get(resultKey); const commit = await state.commit((tx) => tx .check(oldResult) .set(resultKey, output), ); const result = commit.ok ? output : (await state.get(resultKey)).value!; await writeAudit(state, { type: "ATOMIC_COMPLETED", tenantId: call.tenantId, workflowId: call.workflowId, runId: call.runId, action: call.action, commandId: call.commandId, idempotencyKey: call.idempotencyKey, durationMs: ports.clock.now() - startedAt, resultHash: await ports.crypto.sha256( ports.crypto.canonicalJson(result), ), at: ports.clock.now(), }); return result; } catch (error) { await writeAudit(state, { type: "ATOMIC_FAILED", tenantId: call.tenantId, workflowId: call.workflowId, runId: call.runId, action: call.action, commandId: call.commandId, idempotencyKey: call.idempotencyKey, errorCode: classifyError(error), at: ports.clock.now(), }); throw error; } } Exactly-once limitation KV can guarantee that the result record is written once, but it cannot atomically commit with an arbitrary external side effect such as sending an email or charging a card. Therefore: The same logical attempt always uses the same idempotencyKey. External providers must accept and persist that key. The provider must return the original result for duplicate requests. If a provider cannot do this, the action is marked requiresProviderIdempotency: false and the workflow must use a compensating action or an outbox protocol. The unsafe failure window is: external side effect succeeds worker crashes KV result is not written worker retries No workflow engine can eliminate this window without cooperation from the side-effect provider or a transactional resource boundary. 6. Event append with Deno KV CAS class DenoDurableState implements DurableState { constructor( private readonly kv: Deno.Kv, private readonly auditKv: Deno.Kv, ) {} get(key: KvKey): Promise> { return this.kv.get(key); } async *list(prefix: KvKey): AsyncIterable> { for await (const entry of this.kv.list({ prefix })) { yield entry; } } async commit( build: (tx: DurableTransaction) => DurableTransaction, ) { const tx = build(new DenoTransaction(this.kv)); return await tx.commit(); } async appendEvent( run: RunRef, event: WorkflowEvent, ): Promise<{ ok: true; seq: number } | { ok: false }> { const headKey = k.runHead(run.tenantId, run.workflowId, run.runId); for (;;) { const head = await this.kv.get(headKey); const previous = head.value ?? { nextSeq: 0, stateHash: "" }; const seq = previous.nextSeq; const tx = this.kv .atomic() .check(head) .set(k.event(run.tenantId, run.workflowId, run.runId, seq), event) .set(headKey, { nextSeq: seq + 1, stateHash: await hashEvent(event), }); const committed = await tx.commit(); if (committed.ok) { return { ok: true, seq }; } // Another executor appended first. Re-read and retry. } } async appendAudit(record: AuditRecord): Promise { const id = crypto.randomUUID(); await this.auditKv.set( ["audit", record.tenantId, record.at, id], record, ); } async *watch(keys: KvKey[]) { for await (const entries of this.kv.watch(keys)) { yield entries; } } } In production, appendEvent() should also update run metadata and enqueue a wakeup in the same KV transaction when the event is a scheduling event. 7. Workflow definition and DAG A workflow definition is immutable and versioned. interface TemporalConfig { workflowIdReuse: "reject" | "allow-after-terminal"; continueAsNewAfterEvents?: number; continueAsNewAfterBytes?: number; defaultActivityTimeoutMs: number; defaultRetry: RetryPolicy; signalRetentionMs: number; timerSweepIntervalMs: number; } interface DagNode { id: string; kind: "action" | "child" | "timer" | "signal" | "join" | "race"; dependsOn?: string[]; action?: { name: string; version: string; input: Json; compensation?: { name: string; version: string; input: Json; }; }; child?: { workflow: string; version: string; input: Json; }; timer?: { delayMs?: number; at?: number; cron?: string; }; signal?: { name: string; timeoutMs?: number; }; retry?: RetryPolicy; fallback?: string[]; raceGroup?: string; resourceCost?: { cpuMs: number; memoryMb: number; networkRequests: number; }; } interface WorkflowDefinition { name: string; version: string; temporal: TemporalConfig; nodes: DagNode[]; limits: { maxNodes: number; maxDepth: number; maxParallelism: number; maxPayloadBytes: number; maxCpuMs: number; maxMemoryMb: number; }; execute?: ( ctx: WorkflowContext, input: Json, ) => Promise; } Validation occurs before WorkflowStarted is committed: function validateDag(def: WorkflowDefinition): string { if (def.nodes.length > def.limits.maxNodes) { throw new Error("MAX_NODES"); } const ids = new Set(); for (const node of def.nodes) { if (ids.has(node.id)) throw new Error(`DUPLICATE_NODE:${node.id}`); ids.add(node.id); } const indegree = new Map(); const outgoing = new Map(); for (const node of def.nodes) { indegree.set(node.id, node.dependsOn?.length ?? 0); for (const parent of node.dependsOn ?? []) { if (!ids.has(parent)) throw new Error(`UNKNOWN_DEPENDENCY:${parent}`); outgoing.set(parent, [...(outgoing.get(parent) ?? []), node.id]); } } const queue = [...def.nodes] .filter((node) => indegree.get(node.id) === 0) .map((node) => node.id); let visited = 0; while (queue.length > 0) { const id = queue.shift()!; visited++; for (const child of outgoing.get(id) ?? []) { const next = indegree.get(child)! - 1; indegree.set(child, next); if (next === 0) queue.push(child); } } if (visited !== def.nodes.length) { throw new Error("DAG_CONTAINS_CYCLE"); } if (calculateDepth(def) > def.limits.maxDepth) { throw new Error("MAX_DEPTH"); } validateResourceBounds(def); return canonicalHash(def); } The graph hash is stored in the run. A replay cannot accidentally use a different graph definition. 8. Workflow context and deterministic replay The workflow context exposes deterministic commands, not arbitrary effects. interface WorkflowContext { activity( nodeId: string, action: string, actionVersion: string, input: Json, options?: { retry?: RetryPolicy; compensation?: CompensationSpec; }, ): Promise; sleep(nodeId: string, delayMs: number): Promise; waitForSignal(name: string): Promise; child( nodeId: string, workflow: string, version: string, input: Json, ): Promise; continueAsNew(summary: Json): never; version(changeId: string, min: number, max: number): number; } Workflow code must use stable IDs: const orderWorkflow: WorkflowDefinition = { name: "order", version: "3.2.0", temporal: { workflowIdReuse: "reject", continueAsNewAfterEvents: 10_000, defaultActivityTimeoutMs: 30_000, defaultRetry: { maxAttempts: 5, backoffMs: 1_000, maxBackoffMs: 60_000, retryableErrors: ["TIMEOUT", "HTTP_503"], }, signalRetentionMs: 86_400_000, timerSweepIntervalMs: 60_000, }, limits: { maxNodes: 100, maxDepth: 20, maxParallelism: 16, maxPayloadBytes: 1_000_000, maxCpuMs: 300_000, maxMemoryMb: 2048, }, nodes: [ { id: "reserve-inventory", kind: "action", action: { name: "inventory.reserve", version: "2.0.0", input: { sku: "from-input", quantity: "from-input" }, compensation: { name: "inventory.release", version: "2.0.0", input: { reservationId: "from-result:reserve-inventory" }, }, }, retry: { maxAttempts: 3, backoffMs: 500, maxBackoffMs: 10_000, retryableErrors: ["TIMEOUT", "CONFLICT"], }, fallback: ["inventory-fallback"], }, { id: "charge", kind: "action", dependsOn: ["reserve-inventory"], action: { name: "payments.authorize", version: "1.4.0", input: { orderId: "from-input" }, }, }, { id: "notify", kind: "action", dependsOn: ["charge"], action: { name: "email.send", version: "1.1.0", input: { template: "order-confirmed" }, }, }, ], async execute(ctx, input) { const version = ctx.version("payment-provider-v2", 1, 2); const reservation = await ctx.activity( "reserve-inventory", "inventory.reserve", "2.0.0", { sku: input.sku, quantity: input.quantity, }, ); const payment = await ctx.activity( "charge", version === 2 ? "payments.authorize.v2" : "payments.authorize", version === 2 ? "2.0.0" : "1.4.0", { orderId: input.orderId, reservationId: reservation.reservationId, }, ); await ctx.activity( "notify", "email.send", "1.1.0", { template: "order-confirmed", orderId: input.orderId, paymentId: payment.paymentId, }, ); return { reservation, payment }; }, }; 8.1 Replay algorithm class ReplayCursor { private readonly byCommand = new Map(); private readonly versionMarkers = new Map(); constructor( private readonly history: WorkflowEvent[], private readonly results: Map, ) { for (const event of history) { if ( event.type === "ActivityCompleted" || event.type === "ActivityFailed" ) { this.byCommand.set(event.commandId, event); } if (event.type === "VersionMarker") { this.versionMarkers.set(event.changeId, event.version); } } } activityResult(commandId: string, idempotencyKey: string): Json | undefined { const durableResult = this.results.get(idempotencyKey); if (durableResult !== undefined) return durableResult; const event = this.byCommand.get(commandId); if (event?.type === "ActivityCompleted") { return this.results.get(event.idempotencyKey); } return undefined; } wasScheduled(commandId: string): boolean { return this.history.some( (e) => e.type === "ActivityScheduled" && e.commandId === commandId, ); } version(changeId: string, min: number, max: number): number { const existing = this.versionMarkers.get(changeId); if (existing !== undefined) return existing; // The first execution chooses max. That choice is recorded as an event. return max; } } class YieldWorkflow extends Error { constructor(public readonly reason: string) { super(reason); } } The executor loads history and results, then reruns the workflow from its initial input: async function replayAndDrive( def: WorkflowDefinition, run: RunMeta, state: DurableState, registry: AtomicRegistry, ports: PrimitivePorts, ): Promise { const history = await loadHistory(state, run.run); const results = await loadResultsForHistory(state, history); const cursor = new ReplayCursor(history, results); const ctx = new ReplayWorkflowContext( run, cursor, state, registry, ports, ); try { const output = await def.execute!(ctx, run.input); await state.appendEvent(run.run, { type: "WorkflowCompleted", resultHash: await ports.crypto.sha256( ports.crypto.canonicalJson(output), ), }); } catch (error) { if (error instanceof YieldWorkflow) { // An activity, timer, signal, or child is pending. The durable task or // timer record is already responsible for waking this run. return; } if (error instanceof ContinueAsNewSignal) { await continueAsNew(run, error.summary, state, ports); return; } await state.appendEvent(run.run, { type: "WorkflowFailed", errorCode: classifyError(error), }); } } The context implementation is the important part: class ReplayWorkflowContext implements WorkflowContext { constructor( private readonly run: RunMeta, private readonly cursor: ReplayCursor, private readonly state: DurableState, private readonly registry: AtomicRegistry, private readonly ports: PrimitivePorts, ) {} async activity( nodeId: string, action: string, actionVersion: string, input: Json, options?: { retry?: RetryPolicy; compensation?: CompensationSpec }, ): Promise { const commandId = `${this.run.run.runId}:activity:${nodeId}`; const inputHash = await this.ports.crypto.sha256( this.ports.crypto.canonicalJson(input), ); const idempotencyKey = await this.ports.crypto.sha256( [ this.run.run.tenantId, this.run.run.workflowId, this.run.run.runId, commandId, action, actionVersion, inputHash, ].join("|"), ); const result = this.cursor.activityResult(commandId, idempotencyKey); if (result !== undefined) { return result as T; } if (!this.cursor.wasScheduled(commandId)) { await scheduleActivity({ run: this.run.run, nodeId, commandId, action, actionVersion, input, inputHash, idempotencyKey, retry: options?.retry, state: this.state, ports: this.ports, }); } throw new YieldWorkflow(`activity:${commandId}`); } async sleep(nodeId: string, delayMs: number): Promise { const timerId = `${this.run.run.runId}:timer:${nodeId}`; const history = await loadHistory(this.state, this.run.run); if (history.some( (e) => e.type === "TimerFired" && e.timerId === timerId, )) { return; } if (!history.some( (e) => e.type === "TimerScheduled" && e.timerId === timerId, )) { await scheduleTimer(this.run.run, timerId, delayMs, this.state); } throw new YieldWorkflow(`timer:${timerId}`); } version(changeId: string, min: number, max: number): number { return this.cursor.version(changeId, min, max); } continueAsNew(summary: Json): never { throw new ContinueAsNewSignal(summary); } async waitForSignal(name: string): Promise { const signal = await findUnconsumedSignal( this.state, this.run.run, name, ); if (!signal) { throw new YieldWorkflow(`signal:${name}`); } return signal.payload as T; } async child( nodeId: string, workflow: string, version: string, input: Json, ): Promise { const childRun = await ensureChildStarted( this.run.run, nodeId, workflow, version, input, this.state, this.ports, ); if (childRun.result !== undefined) { return childRun.result as T; } throw new YieldWorkflow(`child:${childRun.runId}`); } } A replay never calls an action merely because the workflow code reaches it. It first checks: result KV entry by stable idempotency key; completion event by command ID; pending schedule event; only then creates a new durable task. 9. Durable activity scheduling Scheduling an activity must write the schedule event, task item, and queue message in one transaction. interface TaskEnvelope { taskId: string; queue: string; kind: "activity" | "workflow-wakeup" | "timer" | "signal"; run: RunRef; commandId?: string; action?: string; actionVersion?: string; input?: Json; inputHash?: string; idempotencyKey?: string; attempt: number; availableAt: number; signedCall?: AtomicCall; } async function scheduleActivity(args: { run: RunRef; nodeId: string; commandId: string; action: string; actionVersion: string; input: Json; inputHash: string; idempotencyKey: string; retry?: RetryPolicy; state: DurableState; ports: PrimitivePorts; }): Promise { const taskId = `${args.run.runId}:${args.commandId}`; const queue = "activities"; const event: WorkflowEvent = { type: "ActivityScheduled", commandId: args.commandId, action: args.action, actionVersion: args.actionVersion, idempotencyKey: args.idempotencyKey, inputHash: args.inputHash, }; const task: TaskEnvelope = { taskId, queue, kind: "activity", run: args.run, commandId: args.commandId, action: args.action, actionVersion: args.actionVersion, input: args.input, inputHash: args.inputHash, idempotencyKey: args.idempotencyKey, attempt: 1, availableAt: args.ports.clock.now(), }; await appendEventAndEnqueue(args.state, args.run, event, task); } The queue consumer is at-least-once, so claiming is a CAS operation: interface TaskRecord extends TaskEnvelope { status: "READY" | "RUNNING" | "COMPLETED" | "FAILED"; leaseOwner?: string; leaseUntil?: number; } async function claimTask( kv: Deno.Kv, task: TaskRecord, workerId: string, now: number, ): Promise { const key = k.task(task.queue, task.taskId); const current = await kv.get(key); if (!current.value) return false; if ( current.value.status === "COMPLETED" || ( current.value.status === "RUNNING" && (current.value.leaseUntil ?? 0) > now ) ) { return false; } const next: TaskRecord = { ...current.value, status: "RUNNING", leaseOwner: workerId, leaseUntil: now + 30_000, }; const commit = await kv.atomic() .check(current) .set(key, next) .set(k.taskLease(task.queue, task.taskId), { workerId, expiresAt: next.leaseUntil, }) .commit(); return commit.ok; } A lease sweeper re-enqueues expired tasks. The action result key makes duplicate task execution safe. 10. Timers A timer consists of: a durable timer record; a TimerScheduled event; a delayed KV queue message; a fire CAS operation; a TimerFired event; a workflow wakeup. interface TimerRecord { timerId: string; run: RunRef; fireAt: number; status: "WAITING" | "FIRED" | "CANCELLED"; } async function scheduleTimer( run: RunRef, timerId: string, delayMs: number, state: DurableState, ): Promise { const fireAt = Date.now() + delayMs; const timer: TimerRecord = { timerId, run, fireAt, status: "WAITING", }; const event: WorkflowEvent = { type: "TimerScheduled", timerId, fireAt, }; const task: TaskEnvelope = { taskId: `timer:${timerId}`, queue: "timers", kind: "timer", run, attempt: 1, availableAt: fireAt, }; await appendEventAndEnqueue( state, run, event, task, { delay: delayMs }, ); } The timer consumer: async function fireTimer( kv: Deno.Kv, state: DurableState, task: TaskEnvelope, ): Promise { const timerId = task.taskId.slice("timer:".length); const timerKey = k.timer( task.run.tenantId, task.run.workflowId, task.run.runId, timerId, ); const timer = await kv.get(timerKey); if (!timer.value || timer.value.status !== "WAITING") return; const next = { ...timer.value, status: "FIRED" as const }; const commit = await kv.atomic() .check(timer) .set(timerKey, next) .enqueue({ kind: "workflow-wakeup", run: task.run, reason: "timer", timerId, }) .commit(); if (!commit.ok) return; await state.appendEvent(task.run, { type: "TimerFired", timerId, }); } For recurring Quartz-like schedules: store the recurrence specification in the workflow; materialize the next occurrence as a timer; on firing, compute the next occurrence deterministically; enqueue another delayed timer; use a top-level Deno.cron() job to scan for overdue timer records and repair missed queue deliveries. Deno.cron( "workflow-timer-reconciler", "* * * * *", async () => { await reconcileOverdueTimers(); }, ); The cron job is a repair mechanism. The timer record remains the source of truth. 11. Signals Signals are external durable inputs, separate from the workflow process. Signal ingestion allocates a sequence using a CAS head: interface SignalRecord { signalId: string; workflowId: string; name: string; payload: Json; receivedAt: number; consumedByRunId?: string; consumedAt?: number; } async function deliverSignal( kv: Deno.Kv, tenantId: string, workflowId: string, signal: SignalRecord, ): Promise { const idKey = k.signalById(tenantId, workflowId, signal.signalId); const existing = await kv.get(idKey); if (existing.value) return; // duplicate client delivery const headKey = k.signalHead(tenantId, workflowId); for (;;) { const head = await kv.get<{ nextSeq: number }>(headKey); const seq = head.value?.nextSeq ?? 0; const commit = await kv.atomic() .check(existing) .check(head) .set(idKey, signal) .set(k.signal(tenantId, workflowId, seq), signal) .set(headKey, { nextSeq: seq + 1 }) .enqueue({ kind: "signal", tenantId, workflowId, signalId: signal.signalId, signalSeq: seq, }) .commit(); if (commit.ok) return; } } The signal endpoint must: authenticate the caller; check a capability such as signal:workflow/{workflowId}/{name}; verify the signal ID for deduplication; append to the signal stream; enqueue a workflow wakeup. kv.watch() can watch the workflow signal head, but the signal handler must list the signal stream from the last consumed sequence. It cannot assume each watch notification represents one signal. 12. Child workflows Child creation is a durable transaction: async function ensureChildStarted( parent: RunRef, nodeId: string, workflow: string, version: string, input: Json, state: DurableState, ports: PrimitivePorts, ): Promise<{ runId: string; result?: Json }> { const childWorkflowId = `${parent.workflowId}/${parent.runId}/child/${nodeId}`; const childRunId = await ports.crypto.sha256( `${childWorkflowId}|${version}`, ); const child: RunMeta = { run: { tenantId: parent.tenantId, workflowId: childWorkflowId, runId: childRunId, }, status: "RUNNING", definitionVersion: version, graphHash: await resolveGraphHash(workflow, version), input, parentRunId: parent.runId, createdAt: ports.clock.now(), updatedAt: ports.clock.now(), }; const existing = await state.get( k.runMeta(parent.tenantId, childWorkflowId, childRunId), ); if (!existing.value) { await atomicCreateChildAndWakeParent( parent, child, nodeId, state, ); } const childStatus = await state.get( k.runMeta(parent.tenantId, childWorkflowId, childRunId), ); if ( childStatus.value?.status === "COMPLETED" ) { return { runId: childRunId, result: await loadWorkflowResult(child.run, state), }; } return { runId: childRunId }; } The child has its own event history and can be resumed or replaced independently. The parent waits for a durable child-completion event, not a live process response. 13. Parallel DAG execution, fan-in, fallback, and races 13.1 Fan-out/fan-in For each node, maintain a durable node record: interface NodeState { nodeId: string; status: "PENDING" | "READY" | "RUNNING" | "COMPLETED" | "FAILED"; dependencyCount: number; completedDependencies: number; attempts: number; resultRef?: string; errorCode?: string; } When a node completes: append NodeCompleted; update dependent node counters in the same CAS transaction; enqueue each newly ready node; cap the number of concurrently leased tasks by a durable semaphore or tenant quota. A fan-in node becomes ready only when all required predecessor node states are COMPLETED. 13.2 Retries The retry scheduler computes: function retryDelay(policy: RetryPolicy, attempt: number): number { return Math.min( policy.maxBackoffMs, policy.backoffMs * 2 ** Math.max(0, attempt - 1), ); } Do not use unrecorded random jitter in workflow code. If jitter is required, generate it in the runtime and record the chosen delay in a RetryScheduled event, or derive it deterministically from the command ID. A retry uses: the same logical commandId; the same idempotency key for the side effect; a new attempt number in the task record; a new scheduled retry event. 13.3 Fallback branches A fallback is activated only after the primary reaches a terminal retry decision. The fallback branch is a normal DAG node and is persisted as such: primary failed -> RetryScheduled -> RetryExhausted -> fallback node READY -> fallback action scheduled The workflow must not infer fallback activation merely from a missing task. 13.4 Race conditions For multiple agents competing for one resource: interface LockRecord { owner: string; acquiredAt: number; expiresAt: number; winnerResultRef?: string; } Acquire with: async function tryAcquireRace( kv: Deno.Kv, tenantId: string, lockName: string, owner: string, now: number, ): Promise { const key = k.raceLock(tenantId, lockName); const current = await kv.get(key); if ( current.value && current.value.expiresAt > now && current.value.owner !== owner ) { return false; } const record: LockRecord = { owner, acquiredAt: now, expiresAt: now + 30_000, }; return ( await kv.atomic() .check(current) .set(key, record) .commit() ).ok; } All competitors execute the same CAS. Exactly one sees success. Losers either: wait for the winner’s result; execute a configured fallback; retry after lock expiration. The lock is not the workflow result. The winner must still write a durable result and completion event. 14. Saga and compensation The Core maintains a compensation stack in the run state: interface CompensationSpec { name: string; version: string; input: Json; } interface SagaEntry { completedCommandId: string; compensation: CompensationSpec; } When an activity with compensation completes, the Core appends: { type: "CompensationScheduled", compensationId: "...", action: "inventory.release" } On failure: stop forward DAG scheduling; reverse the completed compensation stack; schedule each compensation as a normal idempotent task; record compensation results; mark the workflow FAILED, COMPENSATED, or COMPENSATION_FAILED. Compensations must be independently retryable and must not rely on the failed agent’s memory. 15. Continue-as-new Continue-as-new prevents unbounded history growth. class ContinueAsNewSignal extends Error { constructor(public readonly summary: Json) { super("CONTINUE_AS_NEW"); } } The transaction is: validate and serialize the summary; create a new run ID; write the new run metadata; append ContinueAsNew to the old run; mark the old run CONTINUED; enqueue the new run; update workflow metadata to point to the new run. The summary must contain only the state necessary to continue. It must not include the entire prior history. async function continueAsNew( oldRun: RunMeta, summary: Json, state: DurableState, ports: PrimitivePorts, ): Promise { const nextRunId = await ports.crypto.sha256( `${oldRun.run.runId}|continue-as-new|${ports.clock.now()}`, ); const next: RunMeta = { ...oldRun, run: { ...oldRun.run, runId: nextRunId, }, status: "RUNNING", input: summary, parentRunId: oldRun.run.runId, continueAsNewFrom: oldRun.run.runId, createdAt: ports.clock.now(), updatedAt: ports.clock.now(), }; await atomicContinueAsNew(oldRun, next, summary, state, ports); } The old run remains queryable and auditable. The workflow ID remains stable while the run ID changes. 16. Workflow versioning A run pins: workflow name workflow definition version graph hash action versions New deployments do not change the definition used by existing runs. For in-place workflow code changes: const version = ctx.version("new-payment-provider", 1, 2); The first execution records a VersionMarker. Every replay returns the recorded value. Old runs therefore continue down the old branch while new runs use the new branch. A definition may be retired only after: no active run references it; all continue-as-new transitions have migrated; a migration handler exists for any persisted summary format. 17. Agent processes, Workers, Sandbox, and WebTransport 17.1 Agent spawning The Gateway owns agent lifecycle. Agents are replaceable executors, not workflow owners. async function spawnAgent( spec: AgentSpec, ): Promise { const command = new Deno.Command(Deno.execPath(), { args: [ "run", "--allow-net=127.0.0.1", "--deny-read", "--deny-write", "agent.ts", ], env: { AGENT_ID: spec.agentId, AGENT_PORT: String(spec.port), ACTION_SET: spec.actionSet.join(","), }, stdout: "piped", stderr: "piped", }); const child = command.spawn(); return { agentId: spec.agentId, pid: child.pid, async close() { child.kill("SIGTERM"); }, }; } The exact Deno permissions are generated from the action sandbox profile, not supplied by the agent. 17.2 CPU isolation For CPU-bound but non-hostile code: const worker = new Worker( new URL("./cpu-worker.ts", import.meta.url).href, { type: "module", name: `activity-${task.taskId}`, }, ); worker.postMessage({ taskId: task.taskId, input: task.input, }); A Worker provides event-loop and memory isolation but should not be treated as a security boundary equivalent to a process or microVM. 17.3 MicroVM execution Use the current Sandbox package: import { Sandbox } from "@deno/sandbox"; async function executeInSandbox( code: string, profile: SandboxProfile, ): Promise { await using sandbox = await Sandbox.create({ allowNet: profile.allowNet ?? [], memoryMb: profile.memoryMb ?? 768, timeout: `${Math.ceil(profile.timeoutMs / 1000)}s`, env: profile.env, }); await sandbox.writeTextFile("/tmp/action.ts", code); const result = await sandbox.sh`deno run --no-prompt /tmp/action.ts`; return JSON.parse(result.stdout); } @deno/sandbox creates an isolated Linux microVM with configurable network allowlists, memory, timeout, environment, and labels. It is appropriate for untrusted custom actions. 17.4 WebTransport and mTLS A Deno WebTransport server has the following shape: const endpoint = new Deno.QuicEndpoint({ hostname: "0.0.0.0", port: 4433, }); const listener = endpoint.listen({ cert: await Deno.readTextFile("./gateway.crt"), key: await Deno.readTextFile("./gateway.key"), alpnProtocols: ["h3"], }); for await (const incoming of listener) { const conn = await incoming.accept(); const transport = await Deno.upgradeWebTransport(conn); await handleAgentTransport(transport); } For production mTLS: terminate QUIC/TLS at a certificate-validating Gateway or QUIC proxy; validate the client certificate chain, SAN, tenant, agent ID, expiration, and revocation; map the certificate identity to an agent capability policy; forward a signed call envelope to the agent; require the agent to return a signed result envelope. The Gateway should not rely solely on the transport certificate. Every atomic call also carries its own signed authorization context. For transports that support HTTP headers, propagate: Authorization: Bearer X-Session-Id: X-Workflow-Id: X-Run-Id: Traceparent: If a particular WebTransport client API does not expose arbitrary request headers, send the same signed envelope as the first message on a bidirectional stream and reject the stream until it validates. 18. Resumable sessions Session state is not workflow state. /workflows/... workflow execution truth /sessions/{tenant}/{sid} agent/user session metadata /sessions/.../events conversation/tool/session events /refreshTokens/... hashed refresh-token records 18.1 Session metadata interface SessionMeta { sessionId: string; tenantId: string; subject: string; status: "ACTIVE" | "REVOKED" | "EXPIRED"; currentAgentId?: string; lastEventSeq: number; createdAt: number; expiresAt: number; } interface SessionEvent { seq: number; type: "USER_MESSAGE" | "AGENT_MESSAGE" | "TOOL_CALL" | "TOOL_RESULT"; payload: Json; at: number; } 18.2 Refresh flow The client presents a refresh token. The Gateway hashes it with the injected crypto provider. It looks up the hash in KV. It checks session, tenant, expiry, rotation state, and revocation. It atomically revokes the old refresh token and writes a new hash. It issues a short-lived signed access token. The access token is sent with every call. Only token hashes are persisted. Refresh tokens are never stored in plaintext. 18.3 Agent replacement When an agent dies: the Gateway marks its lease expired; the harness finds the session’s currentAgentId; it starts a new agent process or Sandbox; it replays session events from lastAcknowledgedSeq; it sends the agent all currently pending workflow signals; it resumes task execution using the same workflow/run IDs; any in-flight atomic task is retried using its original idempotency key. The new agent does not receive a mutable in-memory workflow object. It receives a reconstructed session projection and signed, capability-scoped task envelopes. 19. Gateway and audit security flow For every action: 1. mTLS authenticates the process or agent. 2. Gateway authenticates the access token. 3. Gateway verifies workflow/run ownership. 4. Harness creates a stable command and idempotency key. 5. Registry resolves action and policy. 6. Capability token is checked against action/resource/time/tenant. 7. Canonical call envelope is signed. 8. Runtime executes in the selected Worker/process/microVM. 9. Result is stored by idempotency key. 10. Workflow completion event is appended. 11. Audit record is written to separate audit storage. 12. OpenTelemetry span is ended. Audit records should contain: tenant; subject and certificate identity; workflow/run/command; action and action version; capability ID or token hash; input hash, not secret input; result hash; attempt; sandbox profile; start/end time; outcome; trace ID. The workflow context must have no audit() method and no read access to the audit database. Audit data is out-of-context and cannot affect deterministic replay. For high-assurance deployments, audit writes should be fail-closed for privileged actions: action result -> audit append -> publish workflow completion For ordinary actions, audit can be written asynchronously, but an audit_pending metric and durable retry record are required. 20. Harness entry point export interface HarnessOptions { state: DurableState; registry: AtomicRegistry; definitions: WorkflowDefinitionStore; ports: PrimitivePorts; tenantPolicy: TenantPolicy; } export class DurableWorkflowHarness { constructor(private readonly options: HarnessOptions) {} async start( tenantId: string, workflowId: string, definitionName: string, definitionVersion: string, input: Json, ): Promise { const def = await this.options.definitions.get( definitionName, definitionVersion, ); const graphHash = validateDag(def); validateInputSize(input, def.limits.maxPayloadBytes); await this.options.tenantPolicy.authorizeStart(tenantId, def); const runId = await stableRunId( tenantId, workflowId, definitionVersion, this.options.ports, ); const run: RunRef = { tenantId, workflowId, runId }; await createWorkflowAndInitialRun({ run, definition: def, graphHash, input, state: this.options.state, ports: this.options.ports, }); return run; } async signal( tenantId: string, workflowId: string, signalId: string, name: string, payload: Json, capabilityToken: string, ): Promise { await this.options.ports.permissions.verify( capabilityToken, { resource: `signal:${workflowId}:${name}`, operations: ["send"], }, { tenantId, workflowId, runId: "", action: "workflow.signal", actionVersion: "1", commandId: signalId, idempotencyKey: signalId, input: payload, inputHash: "", capabilityToken, issuedAt: Date.now(), expiresAt: Date.now() + 30_000, nonce: signalId, signature: "", }, ); await deliverSignal( (this.options.state as DenoDurableState)["kv"], tenantId, workflowId, { signalId, workflowId, name, payload, receivedAt: this.options.ports.clock.now(), }, ); } async runWorker(workerId: string): Promise { await listenForTasks( this.options.state, async (task) => { await executeTask( workerId, task, this.options.state, this.options.registry, this.options.ports, ); }, ); } } A production implementation should avoid the private KV access shown in signal() by adding a first-class DurableState.appendSignal() method. The important point is that the harness entry point depends on interfaces, while the Deno adapter contains all concrete KV operations. 21. Testing strategy Each primitive and layer is independently testable. Atomic action tests Use fake ports: const fakeNetwork = new FakeNetwork(); const fakeClock = new FakeClock(1_700_000_000_000); const fakeSigner = new DeterministicEd25519Signer(); const fakePermissions = new AllowListPermissions(); const fakeExecution = new InlineExecution(); Test: capability denial; timeout; input schema rejection; duplicate idempotency key; provider retry; action result replay; secret redaction. Core replay tests Persist a fixture history, then run the workflow twice: const first = await driveWorkflow(definition, fakeState, input); const second = await replayWorkflow(definition, fakeState, input); assertEquals(first.decisions, second.decisions); assertEquals(fakeNetwork.calls, 1); The same action must not be called during replay after its result exists. Crash tests Inject failures at every boundary: before task claim after task claim after external action before result write after result write before event append after event append before queue enqueue after queue enqueue Expected behavior: expired leases are reclaimed; duplicate queue messages are harmless; duplicate external calls use the same idempotency key; completed results are reused; workflow events remain append-only; a new agent resumes from KV. Determinism tests Reject or lint workflow code that uses: Date; Math.random; direct fetch; direct crypto.randomUUID; process environment; uncontrolled iteration over object keys; non-versioned action resolution. The workflow DSL should expose deterministic now, random, activity, sleep, signal, and child APIs backed by recorded events. This architecture gives the harness Temporal-like durable execution while keeping every side-effect boundary replaceable: Deno KV can be replaced with another DurableState, WebCrypto with an HSM signer, Workers with a process runner, Sandbox with another microVM backend, and WebTransport with another authenticated transport without changing workflow semantics.
