Architecture Boundaries (4 Layers + Gateway) ``` ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ Gateway / Zero-Trust Proxy (mTLS + WebTransport + Capability Auth)│ │ - Validates Ed25519/HMAC signatures per request │ │ - Issues capability tokens; maps cert SAN → agent/session identity │ │ - Routes to Agent Runtime (Worker / Sandbox / Process) │ └───────┬──────────────┬──────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┘ │ │ │ ▼ ▼ ▼ ┌──────────────┐ ┌──────────────┐ ┌────────────────┐ │ Main Agent │ │ Sub-Agent │ │ Tool / MCP │ │ (Durable) │ │ (Durable) │ │ Server │ │ WebTransport │ │ WebTransport │ │ TLS/TCP │ └──────┬───────┘ └──────┬───────┘ └────────────────┘ │ │ └────────────────┘ │ ┌─────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ Execution Runtime (Workers, Sandbox.create, Deno.Command, QUIC) │ └─────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │ Atomic invocation envelope (signed) ┌─────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ Atomic Registry (injectable primitives, sandbox profiles, policies) │ └─────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │ Durable interfaces only ┌─────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ Harness Core (DAG compiler, replay engine, version markers, saga) │ └─────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │ DurableState adapter ┌─────────────▼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ Durable State (Deno KV: events, results, timers, signals, locks) │ │ Separate Audit Store (append-only, HMAC-signed, out-of-context) │ └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ ``` Boundary rules: - **Harness Core** never imports `Deno.Kv`, `fetch`, `crypto.subtle`, `Worker`, or `Sandbox`. receives effects via `PrimitivePorts`. - **Atomic Registry** owns primitive resolution (`name@version`), schema validation, sandbox profiles, retry policies, and capability requirements. - **Durable State** only adapter touches `Deno.Kv`. implements `DurableTransaction` with `check/set/delete/enqueue`. - **Execution Runtime** executes primitives selected by Core. manages process replacement when agents die. --- ## 2. Durable State — KV Schema & Transaction Model Use array keys (not slash strings) to avoid ambiguity. ```typescript const k = { workflowMeta: (t,w) => ["workflows",t,w,"meta"], runMeta: (t,w,r) => ["workflows",t,w,"runs",r,"meta"], runHead: (t,w,r) => ["workflows",t,w,"runs",r,"head"], event: (t,w,r,s) => ["workflows",t,w,"runs",r,"events",s], signalHead: (t,w) => ["workflows",t,w,"signals","head"], signal: (t,w,s) => ["workflows",t,w,"signals",s], timer: (t,w,r,n) => ["workflows",t,w,"runs",r,"timers",n], actionResult: (ik) => ["actionResults",ik], task: (q,id) => ["tasks",q,"items",id], taskLease: (q,id) => ["tasks",q,"leases",id], session: (t,s) => ["sessions",t,s], sessionEvent: (t,s,seq) => ["sessions",t,s,"events",seq], refreshHash: (t,h) => ["sessions",t,"refreshTokens",h], raceLock: (t,n) => ["locks",t,n], audit: (t,at,id) => ["audit",t,at,id], }; ``` Event types (immutable append-only): `WorkflowStarted | VersionMarker | ActivityScheduled | ActivityCompleted | ActivityFailed | TimerScheduled | TimerFired | SignalReceived | ChildStarted | ChildCompleted | ChildFailed | CompensationScheduled | NodeCompleted | NodeFailed | ContinueAsNew | WorkflowCompleted | WorkflowFailed` All events appended with `kv.atomic()` comparing `runHead` (`check` previous `versionstamp`, `set` event + new head). guarantees exactly-once event ordering per run. Audit records written to separate `auditKv` (different file path or credential namespace) and signed with HMAC. Workflow code has no read access to audit keys. --- ## 3. Atomic Registry — Injection & Boundaries Every atomic action registered independently of any workflow definition. ```typescript interface AtomicAction { (input: I, ctx: AtomicExecutionContext, deps: ActionDependencies): Promise; } interface ActionSpec { name: string; version: string; inputSchema: Json; outputSchema: Json; capabilities: CapabilityRequirement[]; sandbox: SandboxProfile; // kind: worker | sandbox | command | microvm timeoutMs: number; retry: RetryPolicy; requiresProviderIdempotency: boolean; execute: AtomicAction; idempotencyKey?: (input: Json) => string; } interface SandboxProfile { kind: "worker" | "microvm" | "command" | "in-process"; allowNet?: string[]; allowRead?: string[]; allowWrite?: string[]; env?: Record; memoryMb?: number; timeoutMs: number; region?: string; } class AtomicRegistry { #map = new Map(); // key = "name@version" register(spec: ActionSpec): void { /* validate schemas, sandbox, policies */ } resolve(name: string, version: string): ActionSpec; } ``` Execution uses explicit envelope: ```typescript interface AtomicCall { tenantId: string; workflowId: string; runId: string; commandId: string; idempotencyKey: string; action: string; actionVersion: string; input: Json; inputHash: string; capabilityToken: string; issuedAt: number; expiresAt: number; nonce: string; traceParent?: string; signature: string; // HMAC-SHA256 or Ed25519 over canonical JSON } ``` Before execution: 1. `PermissionPort.verify()` validates capability token against `spec.capabilities`. 2. `DurableState.get(k.actionResult(idempotencyKey))` checks cached result. 3. If cached, return immediately (replay hit) and write audit `ATOMIC_REPLAY_HIT`. 4. If not cached, invoke `ExecutionPort.execute(profile, fn, timeoutMs)`. 5. After success/failure, write result via `kv.atomic()` comparing previous `versionstamp`; only one writer succeeds. 6. Write audit to separate store. unsafe failure window (`external effect succeeds` → `crash` → `retry`) requires either: - external provider accepts `idempotencyKey` and guarantees idempotency; or - workflow uses durable outbox + compensation (Saga). No workflow logic accesses audit data. --- ## 4. Harness Core — Temporal Workflows as Deterministic DAG + Replay ### 4.1 Temporal Configuration & Workflow Definition ```typescript interface TemporalConfig { workflowIdReuse: "reject" | "allow-after-terminal"; continueAsNewAfterEvents?: number; continueAsNewAfterBytes?: number; defaultActivityTimeoutMs: number; defaultRetry: RetryPolicy; signalRetentionMs: number; timerSweepIntervalMs: number; } interface DagNode { id: string; kind: "action" | "child" | "timer" | "signal" | "join" | "race"; dependsOn?: string[]; action?: { name: string; version: string; input: Json; compensation?: CompensationSpec; retry?: RetryPolicy; }; child?: { workflow: string; version: string; input: Json; }; timer?: { delayMs?: number; at?: number; }; signal?: { name: string; timeoutMs?: number; }; retry?: RetryPolicy; fallback?: string[]; raceGroup?: string; resourceCost?: { cpuMs: number; memoryMb: number; networkRequests: number; }; } interface WorkflowDefinition { name: string; version: string; temporal: TemporalConfig; nodes: DagNode[]; limits: { maxNodes: number; maxDepth: number; maxParallelism: number; maxPayloadBytes: number; maxCpuMs: number; maxMemoryMb: number; }; execute?: (ctx: WorkflowContext, input: Json) => Promise; } ``` Validation rules (`validateDag`): - Cycle detection via DFS (`O(V+E)`). - Dependency references exist. - `resourceCost` sums within limits per execution path. - `maxDepth` enforced. - `graphHash` computed deterministically (canonical JSON of sorted nodes/edges). ### 4.2 Replay State Machine harness supports two modes: **functional replay** (re-running `execute()` with replay context) and **direct DAG execution**. For maximum temporal compatibility, replay model uses `ReplayWorkflowContext` throws `YieldWorkflow` to suspend execution at pending effects. ```typescript class ReplayCursor { constructor(private history: WorkflowEvent[], private results: Map) {} wasScheduled(commandId: string): boolean; version(changeId: string, min: number, max: number): number; activityResult(commandId: string, idempotencyKey: string): Json | undefined; } class ReplayWorkflowContext implements WorkflowContext { async activity(nodeId: string, action: string, version: string, input: Json, opts?: { retry?: RetryPolicy; compensation?: CompensationSpec }): Promise { const commandId = `${run.runId}:activity:${nodeId}`; const inputHash = await crypto.sha256(canonicalJson(input)); const idempotencyKey = await crypto.sha256(`${run.tenantId}|${run.workflowId}|${run.runId}|${commandId}|${action}@${version}|${inputHash}`); const cached = this.cursor.activityResult(commandId, idempotencyKey); if (cached !== undefined) return cached as T; if (!this.cursor.wasScheduled(commandId)) { await scheduleDurableTask({ ... }); } throw new YieldWorkflow(`activity:${commandId}`); } async sleep(nodeId: string, delayMs: number): Promise { const timerId = `${run.runId}:timer:${nodeId}`; if (history includes TimerFired for timerId) return; if (!history includes TimerScheduled) await scheduleTimer({ ... }); throw new YieldWorkflow(`timer:${timerId}`); } async waitForSignal(name: string): Promise { const signal = await findUnconsumedSignal(state, run.run, name); if (!signal) throw new YieldWorkflow(`signal:${name}`); return signal.payload as T; } async child(nodeId: string, workflow: string, version: string, input: Json): Promise { const childRun = await ensureChildStarted(run, nodeId, workflow, version, input); if (childRun.result !== undefined) return childRun.result as T; throw new YieldWorkflow(`child:${childRun.runId}`); } version(changeId: string, min: number, max: number): number { return this.cursor.version(changeId, min, max); } continueAsNew(summary: Json): never { throw new ContinueAsNewSignal(summary); } } ``` Replay algorithm for run: 1. Load `runHead` → `nextSeq`. 2. Read all events `0..nextSeq-1` into `ReplayCursor`. 3. Load `actionResults` for all `idempotencyKey` values present in `ActivityScheduled` events into `results` map. 4. Execute `def.execute(ctx, run.input)`. 5. If `YieldWorkflow` thrown, suspend; durable task/timer/signal/child mechanism already responsible for wakeup. 6. If completed, append `WorkflowCompleted` event. 7. If `ContinueAsNewSignal`, execute atomic `continueAsNew()` (create new run ID, write `ContinueAsNew` event, update workflow meta). Replay equality test: ```typescript const first = await driveWorkflow(def, state, input); const replayed = await replayWorkflow(def, state, input); assert.deepStrictEqual(first.decisions, replayed.decisions); assert.strictEqual(fakeNetwork.requests.length, 1); // no duplicate side effects ``` ### 4.3 Determinism Constraints Banned inside workflow functions: - `Date.now()` (use `ctx.now()` backed by event timestamps or deterministic clock) - `Math.random()` (use deterministic PRNG seeded by `run.runId`) - Direct `fetch()` or `crypto.subtle` - Process environment access - Unordered object iteration without explicit sort harness provides deterministic APIs: - `ctx.now()` → reads from replay cursor or event timestamp. - `ctx.random()` → seeded deterministic random. - `ctx.activity()` → only executes via DurableState + AtomicRegistry. --- ## 5. Temporal Features — Concrete Implementation ### 5.1 Signals Signal ingestion (separate from workflow execution): ```typescript async function deliverSignal(kv: Deno.Kv, tenantId: string, workflowId: string, signal: SignalRecord): Promise { const idKey = k.signalById(tenantId, workflowId, signal.signalId); const existing = await kv.get(idKey); if (existing.value) return; // duplicate delivery const headKey = k.signalHead(tenantId, workflowId); for (;;) { const head = await kv.get<{ nextSeq: number }>(headKey); const seq = head.value?.nextSeq ?? 0; const commit = await kv.atomic() .check(existing) .check(head) .set(idKey, signal) .set(k.signal(tenantId, workflowId, seq), signal) .set(headKey, { nextSeq: seq + 1 }) .enqueue({ kind: "signal", tenantId, workflowId, signalId: signal.signalId, signalSeq: seq }) .commit(); if (commit.ok) return; } } ``` `kv.watch()` observes `k.signalHead()` changes, but consumer must `kv.list()` full signal stream from its last consumed sequence (watch may coalesce mutations). ### 5.2 Timers Timer durability uses `kv.enqueue()` with `delay`. `TimerScheduled` event, `TimerRecord` in KV, and queue message with `keysIfUndelivered` committed together. ```typescript interface TimerRecord { timerId: string; run: RunRef; fireAt: number; status: "WAITING" | "FIRED" | "CANCELLED"; } async function scheduleTimer(run: RunRef, timerId: string, delayMs: number, state: DurableState): Promise { const fireAt = Date.now() + delayMs; await appendEventAndEnqueue(state, run, { type: "TimerScheduled", timerId, fireAt }, { taskId: `timer:${timerId}`, queue: "timers", kind: "timer", run, availableAt: fireAt }, { delay: delayMs }); } async function fireTimer(kv: Deno.Kv, state: DurableState, task: TaskEnvelope): Promise { const timerId = task.taskId.slice("timer:".length); const timerKey = k.timer(task.run.tenantId, task.run.workflowId, task.run.runId, timerId); const timer = await kv.get(timerKey); if (!timer.value || timer.value.status !== "WAITING") return; const next = { ...timer.value, status: "FIRED" as const }; const commit = await kv.atomic() .check(timer) .set(timerKey, next) .enqueue({ kind: "workflow-wakeup", run: task.run, reason: "timer", timerId }) .commit(); if (!commit.ok) return; await state.appendEvent(task.run, { type: "TimerFired", timerId }); } ``` `Deno.cron()` job (`"* * * * *"`) scans overdue timer records (`fireAt < now`, status `WAITING`) and repairs missed queue messages. ### 5.3 Child Workflows Child creation writes both parent event (`ChildStarted`) and child run meta atomically: ```typescript interface ChildSpec { nodeId: string; workflowName: string; version: string; input: Json; parent: RunRef; } async function startChild(state: DurableState, parentRun: RunRef, spec: ChildSpec): Promise { const childWorkflowId = `${parentRun.workflowId}/${parentRun.runId}/child/${spec.nodeId}`; const childRunId = await crypto.sha256(`${childWorkflowId}|${spec.version}`); const childMeta: RunMeta = { run: { tenantId: parentRun.tenantId, workflowId: childWorkflowId, runId: childRunId }, status: "RUNNING", definitionVersion: spec.version, graphHash: await resolveGraphHash(spec.workflowName, spec.version), input: spec.input, parentRunId: parentRun.runId, createdAt: Date.now(), updatedAt: Date.now(), }; await state.commit(tx => tx .set(k.runMeta(...childMeta.run), childMeta) .set(k.child(..., childMeta.run), { parentRunId: parentRun.runId, nodeId: spec.nodeId }) .set(k.event(..., 0), { type: "ChildStarted", childRunId, workflowId: childWorkflowId }) .enqueue({ kind: "child-start", ...childMeta.run })); return childRunId; } ``` parent waits via `ReplayWorkflowContext.child()` which checks for `ChildCompleted` event or throws `YieldWorkflow`. child runs independently and writes `ChildCompleted` to its own event history; watch or queue listener copies to parent. ### 5.4 Saga & Compensation Each `DagNode` may declare `action.compensation`. harness maintains durable `compensationStack` per run (stored in KV or derived from event history). On workflow failure: 1. Stop scheduling new nodes. 2. Read completed nodes in reverse order from event history. 3. For each completed node with compensation, schedule `CompensationScheduled` event and durable task. 4. Execute compensation activities via Atomic Registry (with separate idempotency keys). 5. Append `CompensationCompleted` or `CompensationFailed`. 6. Mark workflow `FAILED` or `COMPENSATED` based on compensation results. Compensations must be independently retryable and must not reference failed agent memory. ### 5.5 Race Conditions (Durable Locks) ```typescript interface LockRecord { owner: string; acquiredAt: number; expiresAt: number; winnerResultRef?: string; } async function tryAcquireRace(kv: Deno.Kv, tenantId: string, lockName: string, owner: string, now: number): Promise { const key = k.raceLock(tenantId, lockName); const current = await kv.get(key); if (current.value && current.value.expiresAt > now && current.value.owner !== owner) return false; const record: LockRecord = { owner, acquiredAt: now, expiresAt: now + 30000 }; const commit = await kv.atomic().check(current).set(key, record).commit(); return commit.ok; } ``` Exactly one competitor wins via CAS. Losers either wait (polling `kv.get()` with backoff) or execute configured fallback nodes. winner writes its result to `LockRecord.winnerResultRef` for others to retrieve. ### 5.6 Continue-As-New Limits history growth. Triggered by `ctx.continueAsNew()` or automatically when `continueAsNewAfterEvents` / `continueAsNewAfterBytes` exceeded. ```typescript interface ContinueAsNewSpec { oldRun: RunRef; summary: Json; // minimal state required to continue } async function executeContinueAsNew(state: DurableState, ports: PrimitivePorts, spec: ContinueAsNewSpec): Promise { const newRunId = await crypto.sha256(`${spec.oldRun.runId}|continue-as-new|${ports.clock.now()}`); const newRun: RunRef = { ...spec.oldRun, runId: newRunId }; await state.commit(tx => tx .set(k.runMeta(spec.oldRun.tenantId, spec.oldRun.workflowId, spec.oldRun.runId), { ...currentMeta, status: "CONTINUED", continueAsNewFrom: spec.oldRun.runId, }) .set(k.runMeta(...newRun), { run: newRun, status: "RUNNING", input: spec.summary, parentRunId: spec.oldRun.runId, createdAt: ports.clock.now(), }) .set(k.event(spec.oldRun.tenantId, spec.oldRun.workflowId, spec.oldRun.runId, nextSeq), { type: "ContinueAsNew", nextRunId: newRunId, summaryHash: await crypto.sha256(canonicalJson(spec.summary)), }) .enqueue({ kind: "run-start", ...newRun }) ); return newRun; } ``` new run starts fresh with `runHead` reset; old run remains auditable. --- ## 6. Multi-Agent Runtime & Resumable Sessions Agents replaceable executors, not workflow owners. Workflow truth lives in KV. ### 6.1 Agent Spawning & Replacement ```typescript async function spawnAgent(spec: AgentSpec, ports: PrimitivePorts): Promise { const cmd = new Deno.Command(Deno.execPath(), { args: ["run", "--allow-net=127.0.0.1", "--deny-read", "--deny-write", "agent.ts"], env: { AGENT_ID: spec.agentId, AGENT_PORT: String(spec.port), ACTION_SET: spec.actionSet.join(",") }, stdout: "piped", stderr: "piped", }); return { agentId: spec.agentId, pid: cmd.spawn().pid, close: () => cmd.kill("SIGTERM") }; } async function replaceAgent(sessionId: string, newAgentId: string, state: DurableState): Promise { const session = await state.get(k.session(...)); await state.set(k.session(...), { ...session.value, agentId: newAgentId, status: "ACTIVE" }); // Replay session events + current workflow signals to new agent } ``` Agents expose WebTransport endpoints (`Deno.upgradeWebTransport`) with mTLS (`Deno.listenTls`). Gateway validates certificates and extracts SAN → agent identity. ### 6.2 Resumable Session Flow Sessions independent of workflow runs but reference them. ```typescript interface SessionMeta { sessionId: string; tenantId: string; subject: string; status: "ACTIVE" | "REVOKED" | "EXPIRED"; currentAgentId?: string; lastEventSeq: number; currentRunId?: string; createdAt: number; expiresAt: number; } interface RefreshTokenRecord { hash: string; // SHA-256 of refresh token sessionId: string; rotatedAt: number; } ``` Resumption: 1. Client sends refresh token. 2. Gateway hashes token (`crypto.subtle.digest("SHA-256")`), looks up `RefreshTokenRecord`. 3. Validates session active, rotation state, revocation. 4. Atomically deletes old hash and writes new hash for rotation. 5. Issues short-lived signed access token (`capabilityToken`) for current agent/workflow. 6. Restores session: reads session events from `lastEventSeq`, replays signal history to agent, passes current `AtomicCall` envelopes for any pending workflow tasks. If agent process died, Gateway starts new agent (Worker or Sandbox) and sends reconstructed session projection plus current workflow state (not full context window). ### 6.3 Zero-Trust Security Integration Per Atomic Call Every atomic call carries its own authorization context; no global session object used inside workflow execution. Flow: 1. Client authenticates (Passkey or signed request) → Gateway verifies. 2. Gateway creates `capabilityToken` (signed HMAC/Ed25519) with scopes (`resource`, `operations`, `constraints`). 3. Gateway creates `AtomicCall` envelope with `idempotencyKey`, `commandId`, `capabilityToken`, `signature`. 4. Proxy validates mTLS client cert (`Deno.connectTls` with CA verification) and maps SAN to allowed agent/session. 5. Harness verifies `capabilityToken` against `spec.capabilities`. 6. Runtime executes in selected isolation (`Worker`, `Sandbox.create`, `Deno.Command`). 7. After execution, result stored by `idempotencyKey`; audit record signed and appended to separate store. 8. Telemetry span (`OpenTelemetry`) includes `tenant_id`, `workflow_id`, `run_id`, `command_id`, `action`, `idempotency_key`. --- ## 7. Observability & Telemetry Instrument at `DurableState` adapter level and inside `AtomicRegistry` invocation wrapper. ```typescript interface TelemetryPort { span(name: string, attrs: Record, fn: () => Promise): Promise; event(name: string, attrs: Record): void; gauge(name: string, value: number): void; } ``` Dashboard consumes: - `DurableState` event streams (via `kv.watch()` on run heads or custom projection keys). - Audit records (via `auditKv.list()`). - Metrics pushed via UDP or WebTransport stream (CPU, GPU, token usage, retry counts, lease expirations). Every workflow node produces span tree: `Harness → ReplayLoop → WorkflowExecution → DAGBatch → ActivityExecution → AtomicCall → Sandbox/Worker → NetworkRequest` --- ## 8. Implementation Phases | Phase | Deliverable | |-------|-------------| | 0 | `DurableState` adapter over `Deno.openKv()`; `AuditStore` separate file; core interfaces (`PrimitivePorts`, `DurableTransaction`). | | 1 | `AtomicRegistry` with schema validation, sandbox profiles; register 10+ primitives (`crypto.hash`, `net.request`, `memory.get`, `schedule.enqueue`). | | 2 | `ReplayCursor`, `ReplayWorkflowContext`, replay equality tests; `YieldWorkflow` mechanism. | | 3 | `HarnessCore`: DAG compiler, `validateDag`, `executeWorkflow` (direct mode first, replay mode second). | | 4 | Temporal primitives: `SignalHandler` (delivery + watch), `TimerManager` (enqueue + fire + cron repair), `ChildWorkflowManager` (start + complete propagation). | | 5 | Saga/compensation: durable compensation stack, reverse execution, audit logging. | | 6 | Zero-trust: `ZeroTrustSecurity` (capability tokens, HMAC signing, audit isolation), session/refresh rotation, `replaceAgent`. | | 7 | Gateway: WebTransport server (`QuicEndpoint`, `upgradeWebTransport`), mTLS validation, agent spawning/replacement, proxy routing. | | 8 | Observability: OpenTelemetry spans, dashboard consuming KV projections, metrics endpoint. | | 9 | Hardening: race conditions (`LockRecord` CAS), circuit breakers in runtime, resource limits per sandbox, deterministic replay testing. | --- ## 9. Key Constraints & Design Decisions - **No mutable global state.** Every layer receives interfaces explicitly. - **Every external effect has idempotency key.** Replay skips. - **Every event append-only.** No updates, only new versions via `kv.atomic()`. - **Audit physically/logically separate.** Workflow replay never reads audit. - **Sessions and workflows decoupled.** Agent death does not corrupt workflow state. - **Sandbox isolation configurable.** `Worker` for CPU-bound trusted code; `@deno/sandbox` (`Sandbox.create`) for untrusted custom actions; `Deno.Command` with restricted flags for CLI tools. - **WebTransport (QUIC) agent-agent protocol.** TCP/TLS for external ingress and database connections; UDP only for telemetry. - **Replay equality tested.** Every workflow change requires replay regression test confirming identical `decisions` and zero duplicate side effects. architecture achieves Temporal-level durable execution (resumable, replayable, signal-aware, child-workflow-capable, saga-protected) with atomic-level flexibility (pluggable primitives, sandbox profiles, capability-bound authorization, replaceable storage/network/crypto providers) built entirely on native Deno APIs. ## Elaborate Plan for Agent Orchestration on Deno document provides comprehensive architecture and implementation plan for distributed agent orchestration system built on Deno. system features proxy/gateway, multiple agent nodes (main and sub-agents) on isolated subdomains/ports, toolkits (MCP, file, network), zero‑trust security with mutual TLS, resumable sessions, fine‑grained permissions, secure storage, and full observability. architecture layered to expose clean interfaces, enable open‑source contribution, and support deterministic workflow graphs with fallback and resilience strategies. --- ### 1. High‑Level Architecture Overview ``` ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ User / External Client │ └─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┘ │ HTTPS / WSS ┌─────────────────────────▼───────────────────────────────────┐ │ Proxy / API Gateway │ │ - TLS termination, authentication, routing │ │ - Permission enforcement (zero‑trust) │ │ - Session & state management │ │ - Request/response logging & telemetry │ │ - Internal communication: mTLS, WebTransport, QUIC │ └──────┬────────────┬────────────┬─────────────┬────────────┘ │ │ │ │ ┌───▼───┐ ┌───▼───┐ ┌───▼───┐ ┌───▼───┐ │ Main │ │ Sub │ │ Sub │ │ File │ │Agent │ │Agent │ │Agent │ │Server │ │Port A │ │Port B │ │Port C │ │Port F │ └───────┘ └───────┘ └───────┘ └───────┘ │ │ │ │ └────────────┴────────────┴─────────────┘ │ ┌─────────▼─────────┐ │ Deno KV / Queue │ │ + File System │ │ (encrypted) │ └───────────────────┘ ``` - **Proxy/Gateway**: single entry point for all external and internal requests. handles authentication, authorization, routing, and orchestrates agent lifecycles. - **Agents**: Each agent runs as independent Deno process, listening on its own port/subdomain, exposing secure RPC interface. Agents can spawn sub‑agents. - **Tool Servers**: Dedicated servers for specific capabilities (file access, network, MCP) with their own permission policies. - **Storage**: Deno KV for ephemeral state (sessions, permissions, queues, watch); encrypted files for long‑term logs, artifacts, and key material. - **Observability**: OpenTelemetry + custom dashboard with real‑time metrics, traces, and per‑agent detail views. --- ### 2. Communication Protocols – When to Use Which | Protocol | Use Case | |------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------| | **UDP** | High‑frequency, loss‑tolerant telemetry (e. g., heartbeat, metrics push). Not for control. | | **TCP (plain)** | Internal low‑trust diagnostics or debugging only; never for production (no security). | | **TLS/TCP** | **Default** for all secure, reliable, bidirectional communication between proxy, agents, and tool servers. Use `Deno.listenTls` and `Deno.connectTls` with mutual authentication (client certificates). | | **WebSockets** | Persistent full‑duplex channels for browser clients and for agent‑to‑proxy real‑time event streaming when low latency and message framing important. | | **WebTransport** | Modern alternative to WebSockets over HTTP/3 (QUIC). Use when you need multiplexed streams, low‑latency, and unordered delivery for certain RPCs or media. | | **QUIC** | As underlying transport for WebTransport; can also be used directly for custom protocols needing fast connection establishment and mobility. | **Recommendation**: Use TLS/TCP for all inter‑service communication (agents, proxy, tools). Use WebSockets for browser‑agent interaction. Consider WebTransport for high‑performance, low‑latency agent‑to‑agent streaming if needed. --- ### 3. Zero‑Trust Security Architecture - **Identity**: Each agent, tool server, and proxy has unique **public/private key pair** (Ed25519). Certificates (X.509) issued by internal Certificate Authority (CA) and rotated periodically. - **Mutual TLS**: Every connection requires client certificate verification; proxy validates certificate and maps subject to agent identity. - **Authentication**: User authentication uses passkey (WebAuthn) or public‑key signature. Users sign requests with their private key; proxy verifies signatures. - **Permissions** stored in Deno KV, keyed by (user, agent, resource). They have scopes: - `allowOnce`, `denyOnce` – single use. - `allowSession`, `denySession` – for current session. - `allowAlways`, `denyAlways` – persistent. - `allowUser`, `denyUser` – for user session. - **Session Resumability**: Sessions stored in KV with TTL; each session has unique ID and refresh token. proxy can resume session by validating refresh token. - **Encryption at Rest**: All stored files (logs, artifacts) encrypted using AES‑GCM with keys derived from master key (managed outside, e. g., via environment variable or HSMs). Use `Deno.ensureFile`, `Deno.writeFile` with encryption. --- ### 4. Storage and Logging - **Deno KV** (`Deno.openKv`): - Sessions, permissions, agent registrations, queue items, watched keys. - Use atomic transactions for consistency (e. g., updating permission state). - Watch for changes to trigger workflows (e. g., new task enqueued). - **File System**: - Encrypted logs: structured JSON lines (`std/log`) written to rotating files. - Artifacts (tool outputs, uploaded files) stored in encrypted directories. - Agent checkpoints for resumable sessions. - **Hashing**: - Use SHA‑256 for integrity checks, deduplication (content‑addressable storage), and password hashing (with bcrypt/Argon2 via `node:crypto` or Web Crypto). - Use HMAC for message authentication. - **Logging Strategy**: - All requests, permissions decisions, agent state changes logged to files with correlation ID. - Logs **not** appended to agent’s context to avoid bloat; instead, use logging service can be queried via dashboard. - **Context Summarization**: Periodically compress agent context (conversation history) using summarization model (e. g., via LLM) and store in KV. --- ### 5. Orchestration – Scheduling, Queues, and Workers - **Deno KV + Watch**: Use `kv.watch()` to listen for changes on keys (e. g., new tasks in queue) and trigger handlers. backbone for reactive orchestration. - **Deno Queues**: Built on KV, they provide reliable, at‑least‑once delivery with retries and delay. Use for asynchronous work like sending emails, processing large files, or scheduling agent actions. - **Deno Cron**: For periodic housekeeping (e. g., cleanup expired sessions, rotation of logs, health checks). - **Scheduler**: Implement custom `Scheduler` enqueues delayed tasks using queue with `delay` property, or use `setTimeout` with persistence. - **Workers**: - **Worker Threads** (`new Worker`) – for CPU‑intensive tasks (e. g., encryption, image processing) or to isolate sandboxed code. Each worker runs in separate thread. - **Worker Pool** – manage pool of workers to limit concurrency and reuse them for many tasks. Use shared queue to distribute work. - **Sandboxing**: - Use Deno’s **Sandbox API** (`Deno.createSandbox`) to run untrusted code (e. g., user‑submitted agents) with resource limits, memory caps, and timeouts. - For terminals and browsers, run them inside sandboxed environment with restricted network and file access. --- ### 6. Observability and Telemetry - **OpenTelemetry**: Instrument all components with traces and metrics. Export to collector (e. g., OTLP) or directly to backend (Prometheus, Grafana). - **Metrics**: CPU, memory, GPU (if applicable), network I/O, request latencies, agent state counts. - **Dashboard**: - Built as Deno web app (e. g., using Fresh or React) consumes metrics via REST/WebSocket. - **Agent State View**: Show each agent’s status, current task, input/output, and code‑level console stream (agent thoughts, planning steps, tool calls). - **Timeline View**: Similar to Temporal, visualising workflow execution with start/end times, dependencies, and sub‑workflows. - Use `Deno.perf` and `Deno.memoryUsage` for real‑time system metrics. --- ### 7. Layered Function Design (Atomic → Workflows → DAG) codebase organized in layers, each exposing clear interface. #### Layer 0: Core Utilities - `crypto.ts`: key generation, signing, verification, encryption, hashing. - `log.ts`: structured logging with levels, rotation, correlation IDs. - `kv.ts`: helpers for Deno KV operations (get, set, atomic, watch). - `network.ts`: TLS server/client setup, WebSocket helpers, WebTransport helpers. - `permissions.ts`: permission data structures and evaluation engine. #### Layer 1: Atomic Functions (Low‑level primitives) These standalone, testable functions. | Function | Description | |----------|-------------| | `createAgent(config)` | Creates agent identity (key pair, port, subdomain) and stores in KV. | | `registerAgent(agentId, publicKey, endpoint)` | Registers agent with proxy. | | `startListener(type, port, handler, options)` | Starts TCP/TLS/WebSocket/WebTransport listener. | | `connectTo(endpoint, options)` | Establishes secure connection to another service. | | `sendRequest(conn, request)` | Sends structured RPC request over connection. | | `listenForResponse(conn, timeout)` | Waits for response with timeout. | | `storePermission(subject, resource, action, scope, value)` | Saves permission rule in KV. | | `checkPermission(subject, resource, action)` | Evaluates permissions (with caching). | | `encryptFile(path, data, key)` | Encrypts and writes data to file. | | `decryptFile(path, key)` | Reads and decrypts file. | | `hash(data)` | Returns SHA‑256 digest. | | `generateUUID()` | Creates v4 UUID. | #### Layer 2: Work Actions (Composed Primitives) These combine atomic functions into higher‑level operations. | Function | Description | |----------|-------------| | `createAgentTool(config)` | Creates tool (e. g., file server) with its own permissions and registers. | | `startWaitAndOffloadToSchedule(action, delay)` | Schedules action to run after delay using Deno Queue. | | `startWaitWithListenThenPoll(port, handler, pollInterval)` | Starts listener and polls for incoming requests; returns promise resolves when response received. | | `ProxyVerifyTCPIncoming(conn)` | Verifies client certificate and identity of incoming TCP/TLS connection. | | `ProxySpawnSubAgent(parentAgentId, config)` | Spawns new sub‑agent process with given configuration and passes parent’s public key. | | `ProxySpawnMainAgent(config)` | Spawns main agent with full configuration and permissions. | #### Layer 3: Workflows (Orchestrated Actions) Workflows defined as sequences of work actions with error handling. | Workflow | Description | |----------|-------------| | `DelegateWebSearchToSubAgentAndCheckBack(searchQuery, parentCtx)` | Spawns sub‑agent performs web search; monitors its listener; upon response, validates and continues. | | `DelegateTaskToSubAgentAndPlanForward(task, parentCtx)` | Delegate complex task to sub‑agent; while listening, update plan for next steps. | | `ProcessFileUpload(file, userPermissions)` | Encrypts file, stores, and logs action. | | `HandlePermissionRequest(user, resource, action)` | Presents permission UI and stores decision. | | `ResumeSession(sessionId, refreshToken)` | Validates refresh token and restores session state from KV. | #### Layer 4: DAG and Strategies (Top‑Level Abstraction) Define workflows as **directed acyclic graphs (DAGs)** with nodes being tasks (work actions) and edges denoting dependencies. - **Constraints**: Each task has max duration, retry policy, and fallback. - **Heuristic Check**: Before instantiation, DAG verified for cycles and resource bounds. - **Configuration**: - `allowDelegations`: boolean - `availableTools`: list - `maxTime`: total workflow duration - `maxContextSize`: token limit for summarization - `planningCapability`: which LLM to use for planning - `maxConcurrency`: number of parallel tasks - `cacheStrategy`: e. g., race‑to‑fill cache or use cached results. engine executes DAG, respects timeouts, and implements fallback branches when task fails. --- ### 8. Detailed Function Specifications (Selected) Below key function signatures with brief descriptions. ```typescript // crypto.ts export function generateKeyPair(): { publicKey: Uint8Array; privateKey: Uint8Array }; export function sign(privateKey: Uint8Array, data: Uint8Array): Uint8Array; export function verify(publicKey: Uint8Array, signature: Uint8Array, data: Uint8Array): boolean; export function encryptAES(key: Uint8Array, plaintext: Uint8Array): { ciphertext: Uint8Array; iv: Uint8Array }; export function decryptAES(key: Uint8Array, iv: Uint8Array, ciphertext: Uint8Array): Uint8Array; // kv.ts export async function kvGet(key: Deno.KvKey): Promise; export async function kvSet(key: Deno.KvKey, value: T, ttl?: number): Promise; export async function kvAtomic(op: Deno.AtomicOperation): Promise; export async function kvWatch(keys: Deno.KvKey[], callback: (changes: T[]) => void): Promise; // network.ts export async function startTlsListener(port: number, cert: string, key: string, handler: (conn: Deno.Conn) => void): Promise; export async function connectTls(host: string, port: number, caCert: string): Promise; export function upgradeToWebSocket(request: Request): Promise; export function createWebTransportServer(port: number): Promise; // permissions.ts export type PermissionScope = 'allowOnce' | 'denyOnce' | 'allowSession' | 'denySession' | 'allowAlways' | 'denyAlways' | 'allowUser' | 'denyUser'; export function storePermission(subject: string, resource: string, action: string, scope: PermissionScope, value: boolean): Promise; export function checkPermission(subject: string, resource: string, action: string): Promise; // agent.ts export async function createAgent(config: AgentConfig): Promise; export async function spawnSubAgent(parentId: string, config: SubAgentConfig): Promise; export async function agentListen(agent: Agent, port: number): Promise; export async function agentSendRequest(agentId: string, target: string, request: any): Promise; // workflow.ts export type WorkflowNode = { id: string; action: WorkAction; dependsOn?: string[]; fallback?: string; timeout?: number }; export type WorkflowDefinition = { nodes: WorkflowNode[]; config: WorkflowConfig }; export function validateDAG(definition: WorkflowDefinition): boolean; export async function executeWorkflow(definition: WorkflowDefinition, input: any): Promise; // scheduler.ts export function scheduleDelayed(action: () => Promise, delayMs: number): Promise; export function startCronJob(schedule: string, handler: () => Promise): void; ``` --- ### 9. Important Workflows and Pipelines (Examples) #### 9.1. Web Search Delegation Pipeline 1. **User** sends request to proxy: "Search for X". 2. Proxy authenticates user, checks permission to spawn search agent. 3. Proxy creates **main agent** (if not already) with search capability. 4. Main agent spawns **sub‑agent** on new port, passing search query. 5. Sub‑agent uses tool (e. g., external search API) and listens for responses. 6. While waiting, main agent continues planning with `DelegateTaskToSubAgentAndPlanForward`. 7. Sub‑agent returns results; main agent validates and sends back to user. #### 9.2. Permission Request Flow 1. Agent attempts to access file; proxy checks permission → not found. 2. Proxy triggers `HandlePermissionRequest` which sends prompt to user (via UI). 3. User selects one of scopes (allow once, allow session, etc.). 4. Proxy stores decision in KV and notifies agent. 5. Agent proceeds or fails accordingly. #### 9.3. Resumable Session Workflow 1. User connects, authenticates with passkey → proxy creates session (stored in KV). 2. Session includes refresh token; client receives. 3. If connection drops, client reconnects with refresh token. 4. Proxy calls `ResumeSession`, restores agent context, and continues. #### 9.4. DAG‑based Research Workflow - Nodes: `[FetchSources, Summarize, Analyze, GenerateReport]`. - Dependencies: Summarize depends on FetchSources; Analyze depends on Summarize; GenerateReport depends on Analyze. - Fallback: If FetchSources fails, use cached sources. - Max time: 5 minutes total. - Engine executes with concurrency 1 (sequential) or can parallelize if no dependencies. --- ### 10. Deno Libraries and Features Utilised | Feature / Library | Purpose | |-------------------|---------| | `Deno.serve`, `Deno.listenTls` | HTTP & TLS servers | | `Deno.connectTls` | TLS client connections | | `Deno.serve` with `upgradeWebSocket` | WebSocket servers | | `Deno.serve` with `WebTransport` | WebTransport support | | `Deno.Kv` | Persistent key‑value store with atomic ops, watch | | `Deno.Queue` | Reliable message queue | | `Deno.Cron` | Scheduled jobs | | `Deno.Worker` | Multi‑threading | | `Deno.Sandbox` | Secure microVMs for untrusted code | | `std/log` | Structured logging | | `std/collections` | Data manipulation | | `std/crypto` | Additional crypto helpers | | `std/testing` | BDD testing | | `std/encoding` | Base64, hex, etc. | | `node:crypto` | For compatibility with npm packages | | `@opentelemetry/api` | OpenTelemetry instrumentation | | `@std/async` | Debounce, delay, etc. | | `jsr:@deno/gfm` | Markdown rendering for dashboard | --- ### 11. Security and Resilience - **Rate Limiting**: Use token bucket or sliding window (implement via KV). Apply to all endpoints. - **Exponential Backoff**: For retries (e. g., tool calls), use `@std/async/retry` with custom backoff. - **Graceful Failure**: On error, agent logs failure, cleans up child processes, and releases ports. Use `try/finally` and `AbortController`. - **Cleanup**: Use `Deno.addSignalListener` to catch SIGTERM/SIGINT and perform orderly shutdown. - **Resource Limits**: Each agent has memory limit (via `Deno.memoryUsage` monitoring) and timeouts. --- ### 12. Implementation Roadmap 1. **Phase 0**: Set up project structure, core utilities (crypto, logging, KV helpers). 2. **Phase 1**: Implement atomic functions (Layer 1) with comprehensive tests. 3. **Phase 2**: Build work actions (Layer 2) and integrate with Deno’s networking. 4. **Phase 3**: Create workflow engine (Layer 3 & 4) with DAG validation and execution. 5. **Phase 4**: Develop proxy/gateway and agent spawning logic. 6. **Phase 5**: Implement permissions, sessions, and zero‑trust features. 7. **Phase 6**: Add observability (OpenTelemetry, dashboard). 8. **Phase 7**: Sandboxing and worker pools. 9. **Phase 8**: Hardening, scaling, and performance tuning. --- ### 13. Conclusion architecture leverages Deno’s built‑in capabilities (KV, queues, cron, workers, sandbox, TLS, WebTransport) to build secure, extensible agent orchestration system. layered design ensures low‑level primitives can be reused, workflows deterministic and resilient, and system provides full observability. zero‑trust model with fine‑grained permissions and resumable sessions makes suitable for enterprise‑grade applications. By following plan, implementation will be robust, maintainable, and open‑source ready. extensive use of Deno’s standard libraries reduces external dependencies and keeps codebase lean. Building secure, highly concurrent, zero-trust multi-agent system one of most complex architectural challenges right now. Because you using Deno, you have massive advantage: web-standard cryptography, built-in key-value storage (`Deno KV`), WebTransport, and granular permission flags (`--allow-net`, `--deny-read`) all native to runtime. Here comprehensive architectural blueprint to orchestrate system securely and deterministically. ## 1. Network Topology: Gateway vs. Proxy You should build **API Gateway with Embedded Reverse Proxy**, not just simple proxy. standard reverse proxy (like Nginx) routes traffic blindly. In zero-trust multi-agent network, you need Gateway written in Deno dynamically evaluates state: checking if agent's session revoked in `Deno KV`, verifying WebAuthn passkey signatures, and translating external WebSockets into internal WebTransport streams. ### When to use which protocol: | Protocol | Primary Use Case in Your System | Why? | | --- | --- | --- | | **WebTransport (over QUIC)** | **Agent-to-Agent / Agent-to-Proxy** | ultimate inter-agent protocol. solves "head-of-line blocking." sub-agent can stream 50MB file to proxy on one stream, while simultaneously sending JSON-RPC task updates on another, over same connection. | | **TCP / TLS** | **External Ingress & DBs** | backbone. Use `Deno.serve({ cert, key })` for initial Gateway entry, standard REST toolkits, and connecting to Postgres/SQLite. | | **UDP** | **Telemetry & Observability** | High-frequency, loss-tolerant data. Use `Deno.listenDatagram` for agents blasting CPU/RAM/Network metrics to your monitoring dashboard where dropping packet doesn't matter. | | **WebSockets** | **Browser-to-Gateway** | Full-duplex streams for legacy web frontends and streaming real-time observability console (thought loops, logs) to user's UI. | --- ## 2. Zero-Trust Security & State Do not store logs, sessions, or permissions in LLM's context window— will cause immediate context collapse and token bloat. ### Authentication & Permissions Storage 1. ** User Keyring (Native App):** Wrap your Deno Gateway in lightweight Tauri/Rust app. acts as enclave. stores user's root private key securely in OS Keyring and handles native OS file-picker dialogues. 2. **Session Resumption & Tokens:** When user signs request with Passkey (`WebAuthn`), Gateway issues cryptographically signed capability token (like Macaroon or JWT). 3. **Where to Store State:** Store active session leases, granular permissions (`allow_once`, `deny_agent_session`), and request logs in **Deno KV**. ### Cryptography Routing * **Encryption (`crypto.subtle` AES-256-GCM):** Use to encrypt agent memory dumps or files written to local disk. Never write raw plaintext agent outputs if system multi-tenant. * **Hashing (SHA-256):** Use hashing for deduplication. Hash agent's context or tool inputs to check if cached response already exists. * **Hashing (HMAC-SHA256):** Use to sign internal requests between Gateway and Sub-Agents to ensure external process isn't spoofing internal port. --- ## 3. Orchestration: KV, Schedulers, and Concurrency Your orchestration engine Deterministic Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG). Before execution, system must validate graph for cycles to prevent deadlocks. heuristic check runs in $O(V + E)$ time to ensure no sub-agent waiting on parent agent waiting on same sub-agent. ### State Mechanisms * **Deno KV `watch`:** replaces complex pub/sub brokers (like Redis). When Main Agent spawns task, writes to `["task", "sub-agent-1", "pending"]`. sub-agent uses `kv.watch()` to instantly react when key changes. * **Atomic Transactions (`kv.atomic()`):** Use for locks. If two agents try to access same file router, `kv.atomic().check().commit()` ensures only one succeeds. * **Deno Queues (`kv.enqueue`):** Use for resilient tool execution. Queues natively handle rate limiting, exponential backoff, and dead-lettering if network tool fails. * **Deno Cron:** Use strictly for background cleanup—purging dead sessions, rotating logs, and sweeping orphaned agent data. ### Execution Models | Model | When to use | | --- | --- | | **Worker Threads** | For CPU-bound tasks within agent (e. g., parsing massive PDF, calculating local embeddings). | | **Worker Pool** | To manage LLM harness. You should pool your agents so you don't exceed your system's max concurrency and crash host machine. | | **Sandboxes / `Deno.Command**` | Use `Deno.Command` with strict flags (`--deny-net`, `--deny-write`) to execute Python/JS code written by agent. If on Deno Deploy, use Subhosting MicroVMs. | --- ## 4. Layered Architecture & Function Specifications Here exact codebase structure and function abstraction you need to build. ### Layer 1: Core Gateway & Security Primitives These handle zero-trust validation before any LLM logic runs. * `ProxyVerifyTCPIncoming(req, cert, pubKey)`: Intercepts all traffic. Verifies HMAC signature of requesting agent or Passkey of user. Returns execution context or drops connection. * `ProxySpawnMainAgent(config, permissions)`: Provisions dynamic port, assigns session ID, writes permission bounds to Deno KV, and boots Worker pool. * `ProxySpawnSubAgent(parentKey, scope, tools)`: Spawns agent on new local subdomain, granting subset of parent's permissions (Principle of Least Privilege). ### Layer 2: Async Orchestration & Resilience These handle realities of network failure and timing. * `startWaitAndOffloadToSchedule(task, delay)`: Pushes task to `Deno Queue` to be executed at later time, freeing up agent's thread. * `startWaitWithListenThenPoll(taskId)`: Uses `Deno KV watch` to suspend agent's processing until specific KV key updates (e. g., waiting for user approval on `allow_once` file operation). * `startWaitWithListenThenRequestResilience(tool, maxRetries)`: Wraps tool execution in circuit breaker. Implements jittered exponential backoff if external API rate-limits agent. ### Layer 3: Workflows & Pipelines ( Agent Harness) These functions define standard agent behaviors. * `DelegateWebSearchToSubAgentAndCheckBack(query, context)`: 1. Pauses parent agent. 2. Spawns search sub-agent via WebTransport. 3. sub-agent calls `CallToolContinue()`, evaluates results, and streams data back. 4. Parent agent resumes upon receiving `StreamEnd` signal. * `DelegateTaskToSubAgentAndPlanForward(task)`: Asynchronous fire-and-forget. parent agent delegates task, pipes listener into stack, and immediately moves to next item in its planning phase. ### Layer 4: DAG Compiler & Orchestrator top-level controller. * `CompileAndValidateDAG(workflowNodes, constraints)`: Takes configuration (`max_time`, `max_context`, `tools_available`, `cache_checking_allowed`) and compiles. If graph contains deadlock loop or exceeds max concurrency limits, fails gracefully before spawning single process. * `EmitTelemetrySpan(agentId, metrics)`: Pushes CPU/GPU, token usage, and thought-loop logs via UDP to observability dashboard. > **Key insight:** By decoupling orchestration state (Deno KV) from transport layer (WebTransport) and execution boundary (Workers/Sandboxes), you ensure if one agent hallucinates, crashes, or gets stuck in infinite loop, Gateway can cleanly terminate its Worker and revoke its KV session without taking down main app. exactly direction I think agent frameworks need to evolve. Not " agent calls tools." Rather, **operating system for cognition**, where everything deterministic, composable, observable, resumable and cryptographically verifiable. Reading your notes, I'd actually split system into **12 abstraction layers**, each of which only exposes interfaces upward. implementation remains replaceable. --- # Overall Architecture ``` User │ Passkey Authentication │ ────────────────────────────────────────────── Gateway / Zero Trust Proxy ────────────────────────────────────────────── Session Manager Permission Manager Certificate Manager Audit Logger Router Scheduler Event Bus ────────────────────────────────────────────── Agent Runtime Layer ────────────────────────────────────────────── Workflow Engine ────────────────────────────────────────────── Planning Engine ────────────────────────────────────────────── Memory Layer ────────────────────────────────────────────── Tool Runtime ────────────────────────────────────────────── MCP Servers ────────────────────────────────────────────── Browser Terminal Filesystem Web Databases ────────────────────────────────────────────── Deno Runtime ────────────────────────────────────────────── ``` Every layer only knows layer below. Never upward. --- # Layer 0 Runtime Pure Deno. Use * KV * Queues * Cron * Workers * Sandbox * OpenTelemetry * WebCrypto * WebGPU * WebTransport * HTTP3 * TLS * WebSockets No business logic. --- # Layer 1 Communication surprisingly most important layer. Every communication primitive lives here. Example ``` SendHTTPRequest() SendWebSocket() SendWebTransport() SendUDP() SendTCP() SendTLS() ListenTCP() ListenUDP() ListenWebSocket() ListenWebTransport() OpenQUICConnection() CloseConnection() Reconnect() Ping() Heartbeat() KeepAlive() GracefulDisconnect() ``` No agent logic. Only communication. --- # When to use each protocol ## HTTP Configuration Discovery Authentication Tool invocation Downloads REST One-shot requests --- ## WebSocket Agent streaming Continuous logs Live console Token streaming Dashboard updates Chat NOT for unreliable telemetry. --- ## WebTransport Perfect for agents. Use for bidirectional streams partial reliability parallel streams interruptible transfers video browser control large context movement tool execution live collaboration Eventually becomes your primary protocol. --- ## QUIC Never expose directly. WebTransport runs over QUIC. QUIC endpoint only serves WebTransport HTTP3 --- ## UDP few places. Use only for heartbeat service discovery LAN discovery broadcast metrics fire-and-forget telemetry NOT agent communication. --- ## TCP/TLS Agent-to-agent authentication Certificate exchange secure RPC proxy filesystem router MCP communication Anything requiring guaranteed delivery. --- # Layer 2 Security Everything here Zero Trust. Every request includes ``` Agent ID Certificate Public key Signature Nonce Timestamp Session ID ``` Proxy verifies ``` VerifySignature() VerifyCertificate() VerifyPermission() VerifyNonce() VerifySession() VerifyExpiration() VerifyRevocation() ``` Never trust localhost. Treat localhost exactly like internet. --- # Layer 3 Identity Objects ``` Agent User Proxy Tool MCP Server Worker Sandbox ``` Each gets ``` UUID Keypair Certificate Capabilities Permissions Metadata ``` Functions ``` CreateKeypair() RotateKeys() IssueCertificate() RevokeCertificate() ExportPublicKey() ImportPublicKey() ``` --- # Layer 4 Permissions Every permission becomes ``` Capability Token ``` Example ``` Read Files Write Files Spawn Agent Open Browser Internet Network GPU Terminal Clipboard Memory Microphone Camera ``` Permission functions ``` RequestPermission() GrantPermission() DenyPermission() StoreDecision() ExpireDecision() RevokePermission() ``` Decision storage ``` Allow Once Allow Session Allow Agent Session Allow Always Deny Once Deny Session Deny Always ``` Exactly as you described. --- # Layer 5 Storage Never mix storage. Split. ## Deno KV Session state Agent state Workflow state Locks Queues Small metadata Permission cache Heartbeat --- SQLite Logs Memory Context Conversations Indexes Embeddings metadata --- Blob storage Files Images Contexts Snapshots Large prompts Artifacts --- Vector DB Semantic memory Retrieval Embeddings --- # Layer 6 Scheduler Functions ``` Schedule() Cancel() Pause() Resume() Delay() Retry() Backoff() Timeout() Heartbeat() Watch() AwaitSignal() Poll() ``` Internally KV Queue Cron Worker --- Use Queue when background execution --- Use Cron when time based --- Use KV Watch when event driven --- # Layer 7 Workers Worker Thread CPU intensive Parsing Embeddings Compression Encryption Hashing Planning Graph compilation --- Worker Pool Multiple independent jobs 100 browser tasks 100 embeddings 100 searches Many tool invocations --- Subprocess Terminal Git Python Node CLI Sandbox --- Sandbox Never trust Generated code Python Terminal npm External code --- # Layer 8 Agent Runtime Functions ``` CreateAgent() DestroyAgent() PauseAgent() ResumeAgent() SleepAgent() WakeAgent() RestartAgent() SnapshotAgent() CloneAgent() ForkAgent() MergeAgent() KillAgent() ``` --- Subagent ``` SpawnSubAgent() VerifySpawn() AssignTask() CancelTask() WaitForResult() JoinResult() DisposeSubAgent() ``` --- # Layer 9 Tool Runtime Every tool ``` Registered Signed Sandboxed Permissioned Observable Versioned ``` Functions ``` RegisterTool() CallTool() CancelTool() RetryTool() VerifyOutput() StreamOutput() CacheOutput() ``` --- # Layer 10 Workflow Engine where your idea becomes unique. Everything becomes DAG. Example ``` User ↓ Plan ↓ Spawn Search Agent ↓ Search ↓ Verify ↓ Summarize ↓ Return ``` Every node ``` Timeout Retries Fallback Expected output Validator Metrics ``` Compile ↓ Graph ↓ Static Analysis ↓ Cycle Detection ↓ Deadlock Detection ↓ Memory Check ↓ Resource Check ↓ Permission Check ↓ Only then execute. --- # Workflow primitives ``` Sequence Parallel Race Loop Retry Branch Merge FanOut FanIn Wait Join Reduce Scatter Barrier Mutex Semaphore ``` --- # Higher abstractions Exactly what you described. Example ``` DelegateSearchWorkflow() DelegateCodingWorkflow() DelegatePlanningWorkflow() DelegateBrowserWorkflow() DelegateResearchWorkflow() DelegateFilesystemWorkflow() ``` Each internally calls ``` Spawn Authenticate Schedule Listen Wait Validate Summarize Merge Dispose ``` --- # Planning Layer Planner never executes. Planner only builds DAG. Functions ``` Think() Estimate() Forecast() BreakTask() ScorePlan() EvaluateRisk() GenerateFallback() PredictFailure() EstimateCost() ``` --- # Execution Layer Never think. Only execute. ``` ExecuteNode() ExecuteGraph() ExecuteBranch() ExecuteParallel() ExecuteRetry() ``` --- # Memory Layer Split memory. Working Session Long Term Semantic Episodic Tool Cache Conversation Scratchpad --- Context should never grow. Pipeline ``` Raw Context ↓ Summarizer ↓ Compression ↓ Memory ↓ Retrieval ↓ Fresh Context ``` No append-only prompts. --- # Logging Everything. Everything. Agent Workflow Permission CPU GPU Memory Tool Latency Retries Errors Context Planning Execution Tokens Costs --- Store separately. Never in context. --- Telemetry OpenTelemetry Each node creates spans. ``` Agent ↓ Workflow ↓ Tool ↓ Browser ↓ Filesystem ↓ LLM ``` Timeline becomes automatic. --- Dashboard Almost Temporal. Views Agent Tree Workflow DAG Timeline Logs CPU GPU Memory Queues KV Workers Network WebSockets WebTransport Token Usage Context Retries Permissions Certificates --- Console Every node ``` stdout stderr thinking planning reasoning summary tool logs network memory ``` --- # Hashing Use hashes for Artifact IDs Context IDs Cache Keys Prompt IDs Workflow IDs Permission snapshots Configuration fingerprints Binary verification Audit chains Use digital signatures—not hashes alone—for identity and authorization. --- # Resilience Every node ``` Timeout ↓ Retry ↓ Exponential Backoff ↓ Circuit Breaker ↓ Fallback ↓ Escalation ↓ Human ``` --- # Atomic primitive functions I would aim for roughly 250-400 primitives grouped into small interfaces rather than handful of large functions. Some representative groups: * **Identity:** `CreateAgent`, `IssueCertificate`, `RotateKeys`, `VerifySignature` * **Networking:** `ConnectTLS`, `OpenWebTransportSession`, `ListenWebSocket`, `SendFrame` * **Scheduling:** `EnqueueTask`, `ScheduleAt`, `DelayUntil`, `CancelSchedule` * **Synchronization:** `AcquireLease`, `ReleaseLease`, `WaitForSignal`, `PublishEvent` * **Persistence:** `KVGet`, `KVSet`, `KVAtomicCommit`, `AppendAuditRecord` * **Workflow:** `CreateNode`, `LinkNodes`, `ValidateDAG`, `CompileWorkflow`, `ExecuteNode` * **Planning:** `GeneratePlan`, `EstimateResources`, `SelectStrategy`, `GenerateFallbackPlan` * **Memory:** `SummarizeContext`, `StoreMemory`, `RetrieveMemory`, `EvictWorkingSet` * **Security:** `AuthorizeCapability`, `VerifyNonce`, `CreateSession`, `ResumeSession` * **Observability:** `StartSpan`, `RecordMetric`, `StreamLogs`, `CaptureSnapshot` Each primitive should do **one thing only**, making higher-level workflows deterministic, testable, and reusable. ## architectural principle biggest shift I'd make compared to today's agent frameworks to stop treating "agents" as primary abstraction. Instead: ``` Cryptographic Identity ↓ Capabilities ↓ Deterministic Workflows (DAG) ↓ Execution Runtime ↓ Agents (stateless executors) ↓ Tools ``` In model, agents become **replaceable workers**, while workflow graph, permissions, identity, and state become durable system. makes pausing, resuming, migrating, replaying, auditing, and even replacing LLM implementation straightforward without changing orchestration itself. much closer to designing distributed operating system than building another agent framework, and naturally accommodates Deno's strengths: Web APIs, WebCrypto, KV, Queues, Cron, OpenTelemetry, Sandboxes, Workers, WebTransport, and HTTP/3. 0. Deno API constraints affect design Two Deno details important: Deno KV queues at-least-once, not exactly-once. handler must use durable task/result record and idempotency key. kv. enqueue() supports delayed delivery, retry backoff, and keysIfUndelivered; can also be included in kv. atomic() transactions. Deno KV queue documentation(opens in new tab) kv. watch() wake-up mechanism, not event-log reader. may coalesce multiple mutations and only delivers latest state. executor must reread ordered events from KV after receiving watch notification. Deno KV watch documentation(opens in new tab) current Sandbox API Sandbox. create() from @deno/sandbox; not Deno. createSandbox(). Deno Sandbox documentation(opens in new tab) WebTransport server support currently unstable and uses Deno. QuicEndpoint, listener. accept(), and Deno. upgradeWebTransport(). Deno WebTransport example(opens in new tab) Deno. cron() recurring scheduler. In CLI, its execution state process-local; on Deploy, Deno schedules registered jobs. should be reconciliation mechanism, not primary storage for individual workflow timers. Deno Cron documentation(opens in new tab) durable semantics below therefore come from KV records, CAS transactions, immutable events, and idempotent task execution—not from queue delivery, process lifetime, watch(), or Deno. cron() alone. 1. Architecture ┌────────────────────────────┐ │ Gateway / Proxy │ │ mTLS, tenant auth, routing │ └──────────────┬─────────────┘ │ WebTransport / HTTP3 ┌──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┐ │ │ │ ┌────────▼────────┐ ┌────────▼────────┐ ┌────────▼────────┐ │ Main Agent │ │ Sub-Agent │ │ Ephemeral Agent │ │ process │ │ process │ │ Sandbox/Worker │ └──────────────────┘ └─────────────────┘ └─────────────────┘ │ ┌───────────────────────────────────────▼────────────────────────────────────┐ │ Execution Runtime │ │ task leasing, process supervision, WebTransport, Worker/Sandbox execution │ └───────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┘ │ ┌───────────────────────────────────────▼────────────────────────────────────┐ │ Atomic Registry │ │ action metadata, capability requirements, provider resolution, invocation │ └───────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┘ │ ┌───────────────────────────────────────▼────────────────────────────────────┐ │ Harness Core │ │ deterministic workflow/DAG state machine, replay, retries, timers, signals │ │ child workflows, saga, fan-out/fan-in, races, continue-as-new │ └───────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┘ │ DurableState interface ┌───────────────────────────────────────▼────────────────────────────────────┐ │ Durable State │ │ Deno KV workflow state, events, results, tasks, timers, sessions, signals │ └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ ┌────────────────────────────┐ │ Separate Audit Store │ │ audit KV / append-only file │ └────────────────────────────┘ 1.1 Boundary rules Harness Core Pure orchestration. must not directly import: Deno. openKv() fetch crypto. subtle Date. now() Math. random() Deno. Command Worker Sandbox process globals agent connection state receives all effects through interfaces. Its responsibilities: validate and hash workflow definitions; construct deterministic command IDs; execute workflow state machine; execute DAG scheduling decisions; replay event history; decide retry, fallback, compensation, and continue-as-new; handle workflow-level signals and timers; generate child-workflow commands; enforce deterministic version markers. Atomic Registry registry not agent class and not workflow implementation. contains independently registered atomic operations and their policies: input/output schemas; action implementation; provider dependencies; required capabilities; sandbox profile; timeout; retry policy; idempotency requirements; resource limits; version. only layer allowed to resolve action name to executable code. Durable State adapter around Deno KV or another implementation. owns: KV reads and writes; optimistic CAS; immutable event append; task creation and leases; timer records; signal records; action results; session records; watch subscriptions. Core sees DurableState, not Deno. Kv. Execution Runtime runtime performs effects selected by Core: task polling and leasing; action invocation; CPU-bound Worker execution; isolated command execution; @deno/sandbox execution; process spawning and replacement; WebTransport communication; mTLS gateway integration; OpenTelemetry spans; retry wakeups. runtime does not own workflow truth. If agent disappears, runtime loses only its lease; workflow remains in KV. 2. Core interfaces type Json = | null | boolean | number | string | Json[] | { [key: string]: Json }; type KvKey = readonly (string | number | bigint | Uint8Array)[]; interface Entry { key: KvKey; value: T | null; versionstamp: string | null; } interface DurableState { get(key: KvKey): Promise>; list(prefix: KvKey): AsyncIterable>; /** * Executes KV CAS transaction. callback must build operation * from values read before callback. */ commit( operation: (tx: DurableTransaction) => DurableTransaction, ): Promise<{ ok: true; versionstamp: string } | { ok: false }>; appendEvent( run: RunRef, event: WorkflowEvent, ): Promise<{ ok: true; seq: number } | { ok: false }>; appendAudit(record: AuditRecord): Promise; watch(keys: KvKey[]): AsyncIterable[]>; } interface DurableTransaction { check(entry: Entry): DurableTransaction; set(key: KvKey, value: unknown): DurableTransaction; delete(key: KvKey): DurableTransaction; enqueue( message: unknown, options?: { delay?: number; backoffSchedule?: number[]; keysIfUndelivered?: KvKey[]; }, ): DurableTransaction; } interface RunRef { tenantId: string; workflowId: string; runId: string; } interface PrimitivePorts { crypto: CryptoPort; signer: SignerPort; clock: ClockPort; network: NetworkPort; scheduler: SchedulerPort; execution: ExecutionPort; memory: MemoryPort; permissions: PermissionPort; telemetry: TelemetryPort; } interface ClockPort { now(): number; } interface CryptoPort { randomBytes(length: number): Promise; sha256(value: Uint8Array | string): Promise; canonicalJson(value: unknown): string; } interface SignerPort { sign(input: Uint8Array): Promise; verify(input: Uint8Array, signature: string, keyId: string): Promise; } interface NetworkPort { request( request: { url: string; method: string; headers: Record; body?: Uint8Array; }, policy: NetworkPolicy, ): Promise<{ status: number; headers: Record; body: Uint8Array; }>; } interface SchedulerPort { enqueue(task: TaskEnvelope, delayMs?: number): Promise; cancel(timerId: string): Promise; } interface ExecutionPort { execute( profile: SandboxProfile, fn: () => Promise, timeoutMs: number, ): Promise; spawnAgent(spec: AgentSpec): Promise; replaceAgent(agentId: string, spec: AgentSpec): Promise; } interface MemoryPort { get(key: string): Promise; put(key: string, value: T): Promise; } interface PermissionPort { verify( capabilityToken: string, requirement: CapabilityRequirement, call: AtomicCall, ): Promise; } interface TelemetryPort { span(name: string, attributes: Record, fn: () => Promise): Promise; } PrimitivePorts constructed once for process, but passed explicitly. No workflow or atomic action obtains them from module-level mutable state. 3. KV key model following logical paths. In Deno KV they should be represented as arrays, not slash-concatenated strings. const k = { workflow: (tenant: string, workflowId: string) => ["workflows", tenant, workflowId] as const, run: (tenant: string, workflowId: string, runId: string) => ["workflows", tenant, workflowId, "runs", runId] as const, runMeta: (tenant: string, workflowId: string, runId: string) => ["workflows", tenant, workflowId, "runs", runId, "meta"] as const, runHead: (tenant: string, workflowId: string, runId: string) => ["workflows", tenant, workflowId, "runs", runId, "head"] as const, event: (tenant: string, workflowId: string, runId: string, seq: number) => ["workflows", tenant, workflowId, "runs", runId, "events", seq] as const, signalHead: (tenant: string, workflowId: string) => ["workflows", tenant, workflowId, "signals", "head"] as const, signal: (tenant: string, workflowId: string, seq: number) => ["workflows", tenant, workflowId, "signals", seq] as const, signalById: (tenant: string, workflowId: string, signalId: string) => ["workflows", tenant, workflowId, "signalsById", signalId] as const, task: (queue: string, taskId: string) => ["tasks", queue, "items", taskId] as const, taskLease: (queue: string, taskId: string) => ["tasks", queue, "leases", taskId] as const, actionResult: (idempotencyKey: string) => ["actionResults", idempotencyKey] as const, timer: (tenant: string, workflowId: string, runId: string, timerId: string) => ["workflows", tenant, workflowId, "runs", runId, "timers", timerId] as const, child: (tenant: string, parentRunId: string, childId: string) => ["children", tenant, parentRunId, childId] as const, session: (tenant: string, sessionId: string) => ["sessions", tenant, sessionId] as const, sessionEvent: (tenant: string, sessionId: string, seq: string, lockName: string) => ["locks", tenant, lockName] as const, }; audit store must use different KV database handle or separate append-only storage namespace inaccessible to workflow code: const auditKv = await Deno. openKv("./audit. kv"); const workflowKv = await Deno. openKv("./workflow. kv"); For hosted deployment, these should preferably be separate databases or separate storage credentials, not merely prefix in same database. 3.1 Workflow metadata interface WorkflowMeta { tenantId: string; workflowId: string; status: "RUNNING" | "COMPLETED" | "FAILED" | "CANCELLED"; currentRunId: string; definitionName: string; definitionVersion: string; graphHash: string; createdAt: number; updatedAt: number; } interface RunMeta { run: RunRef; status: "RUNNING" | "WAITING" | "COMPLETED" | "FAILED" | "CONTINUED"; definitionVersion: string; graphHash: string; input: Json; parentRunId?: string; continueAsNewFrom?: string; createdAt: number; updatedAt: number; } interface RunHead { nextSeq: number; stateHash: string; } 3.2 Events type WorkflowEvent = | { type: "WorkflowStarted"; run: RunRef; input: Json; definitionVersion: string; graphHash: string; } | { type: "VersionMarker"; changeId: string; version: number; } | { type: "ActivityScheduled"; commandId: string; action: string; actionVersion: string; idempotencyKey: string; inputHash: string; } | { type: "ActivityCompleted"; commandId: string; idempotencyKey: string; resultHash: string; } | { type: "ActivityFailed"; commandId: string; attempt: number; retryable: boolean; errorCode: string; } | { type: "TimerScheduled"; timerId: string; fireAt: number; } | { type: "TimerFired"; timerId: string; } | { type: "SignalReceived"; signalId: string; name: string; payloadHash: string; } | { type:
